## Dungeons & Dragons Online/The Druid's Deep

The Druid's Deep is an adventure pack for Dungeons & Dragons Online where the player characters must investigate a corrupted druid grove. The adventure pack was added to the game on August 20, 2012 as part of Update 15.

Plot

The Druid's Deep takes place at the same time as the events of Menace of the Underdark, and shortly after the events of the Shadow Over Wheloon section of Shadowfell Conspiracy. Like The High Road of Shadows, it follows a plot tied to Oriphaun Huntsilver's flight from Wheloon Prison with the Abyssal Planus Mechanus Nether Scroll, and deals with the repercussions of his seemingly insignificant act of passing on a magical staff he found to his friend Halsaime. The priest Anazar Narroun serves as the central questgiver for the adventure as he directs the player to investigate each issue as they emerge.

Outbreak is introduced by the priestess Meena Follakin, who sends the player to the Eveningstar War Hospital to address a bizarre plague afflicting it. With the ongoing war against the drow, the Purple Dragons and War Wizards are unable to get involved, and so it falls to whoever volunteers to help. It is revealed that the ranger Peron Uthe carried a strange disease from the druids of Thorngap, and has caused many patients to become red musk zombies. The head priest Hurim Jaskyr uses samples from the recently-converted zombies Mikhael Jarvin and Aeron Leifbrand to create an inoculation against the plague, and Peron Uthe is destroyed before this zombie plague can escape the hospital.

Overgrowth follows on with Eolynn Arva, who reports spotting strange plant growth emerging from Peron's home. The investigation reveals that the building is filled with unnatural plant growth and corrupted, murderous creatures, led by the dryad Sylvie. Meeting the Harper Jussko Kwahn, they team up to take the dryad out before she can threaten the whole town.

Thorn and Paw has the player working with the Harper Kirinel Zay to track down the origin of this strange plant corruption. She sends them with Garstrom Toom to find Thorn Hollow, where Halsaime lives, only to find the druid circle filled with more hostile plants, frenzied animals, and mad druids. They find the friendly druid Carm Woodrusk, who reveals that Halsaime is responsible for the nightmare, and gives a cryptic hint as to where to find him before dying of her injuries.

The Druid's Curse is directed by the Harper Tarim Inavule, who sends the player on a final strike to the Druid's Deep where Halsaime has hidden. Fighting through his last defenders, Halsaime is caught and killed, with Jussko Kwahm destroying the powerful cursed staff that drove him mad in the first place. Pleased with saving the day, Tarim promises to immortalize the player's victory in a new song, The Song of Druid's Deep.

## Dunfield

Captain Dunfield was a mercenary officer who operated throughout the Sword Coast region of the North during the late 15 century DR.

Personality
Dunfield was considered a hardened and distrusting man who was somehow able to remain honorable during the difficult times in which he lived. He always did his best by the soldiers under his command and strived to serve the community at large.

Relationships
He had a long-standing relationship with Dagult Neverember, Lord Protector of Neverwinter.

History
After Lord Neverember started the New Neverwinter movement and rose to power in Neverwinter during the 1470s DR, he appointed Dunfield as captain of the town guard of Helm's Hold and made the official decree of Protector's Law, stating that all spellscarred residents of the city would be sent to the Hold for treatment. While Captain Dunfield kept the peace in the Helmite city a new prophet of the Watcher named Rohini rose to great acclaim for her treatment of the unfortunate spellscarred. Some time before the, Rohini revealed herself as a succubus and the leader of the devil-worshiping Ashmadai. She gathered her Ashmadai cult followers and drove Dunfield and his soldiers out from the city, forcing them to regroup elsewhere.

Captain Dunfield rallied what soldiers and mercenaries he had at a small camp outside Helm's Hold, known as the Last Redoubt. They built siege weapons and marched on the city, initiating the Siege of Helm's Hold. In response, the Prophet Rohini and her cultists summoned scores of devils from the Nine Hells and rebuffed the assault, keeping Dunfield and the soldiers of the Neverwinter Guard out of the city.

After seeking aid from Sergeant Knox in Neverwinter, Captain Dunfield worked with some adventurers, including the Hero of Sleeping Dragon Bridge, to reclaim Helm's Hold. He directed them to slay the imps, legion devils and hellfire warlocks that roamed the battlefield, and then rally with the beleaguered Brother Satarin stuck in the city.

## Dust wight

Dust wights were a type of undead creature that formed from conjunctions of elemental earth and negative energy.

Description
These resembled earth elementals, albeit the size of a human and with gaunt forms. They were always surrounded by a small cloud of dust and despite their appearance they weighed around .

Behavior
Dust wights sought to destroy all things constructed of metal or stone. They were pleased whenever it was necessary to kill living creatures in order to accomplish this.

Abilities
Creatures or objects constructed of metal or store, such as shield guardians and stone golems, would be weakened when bashed by the fists of a dust wight. In addition, the dust clouds that surrounded them had a petrification effect.

Dust wights were equally affected by turn undead and spells that rebuked creatures of elemental earth.

History
In the, a group of adventurers discovered some dust wights within an abandoned guardhouse inside the tomb of the Netherese scholar Augathra the Mad.

Ecology
Languages
Dust wights only knew the Terran language.

## Dumal Erard

First Guardian Dumal Erard was a paladin-priest of Helm, and an adventuring member of the Company of Crazed Venturers who later went on to found the community of Helm's Hold during the 14 century DR.

Personality
Quite fitting for a paladin of Helm, Dumal was noted for being a strong war-time leader.

Activities
For years Dumal and his company ventured around the Sword Coast North, based out of the great metropolis of Waterdeep.

Relationships
Among his fellow Company of Crazed Venturers were the skilled fighter Loene; the repeatedly-slain-and-raised wizard Nain Keenwhistler, the still-deceased warrior Tzarrakyn the elder; Filiare, founder of the Inn of the Dripping Dagger, Trunnigan Regallis, one of the few humans allowed to live on Evermeet; Tolgar Anuvien, the founder of Goldenfields; and two wizards of the Harpers, Savengriff and Malchor Harpell.

History
At some point in his career, Dumal defeated Iltmul at the Citadel of the Mists and attained the tile of White Master of Dragons. He later defended the title against the monk known as Hlam.

After Dumal settled down from his life as an adventurer, he relocated to the farming community known as Helm's Stead and began work on a great monastery dedicated to his patron deity. By, he and his allies managed to officially found the fortified temple of Helm's Hold. He defended his title as First Guardian and ruler of the city at least six times over several decades after its founding.

## Durakh Haan

Durakh Haan was a devout cleric of Luthic who dwelled within the ruins of Citadel Rashemar during the late 14 century DR.

While she was clearly a half-orc, exact specifics of her human and orcish heritage was unknown.

Personality
She was known for her rather acidic personality.

Abilities
Durakh studied the practice of place magic under her mistress Chaul, a malevolent durthan who ruled over the ruined citadel.

Activities
To aid the efforts of her warband, Durakh would raise the fallen warriors and animate the remains of their slain foes. She commanded her undead minions to stand guard over the citadel's inner domain.

She was the most trusted ally of Chaul, serving as her chief lieutenant of the Warband of the Citadel.

## Dungeons & Dragons Dice Masters: Tomb of Annihilation

Dungeons & Dragons Dice Masters: Tomb of Annihilation is a dice-building game set in Forgotten Realms. It utilizes WizKids Games' proprietary Dice Building Game platform in which two players take the role of warlords, collect and assemble their "party" of adventurer dice and battle in head-to-head gameplay.

Gameplay
Each turn, players roll the dice to see what resources they have available, buy dice, send their party members into the field, and then strike at the enemy warlord. There are multiple cards available for each type of die so players get to choose which one to use. This lets players specialize dice to suit play style.

## Du'Quelve Hunzrin

Du'Quelve Hunzrin was a priestess of House Hunzrin in Menzoberranzan in the early 14 century DR.

Activities
Du'Quelve was often ordered by Matron Arolina Hunzrin to lead trade expeditions outside Menzoberranzan.

History
In the, during one of these missions, the Hunzrin met two priestesses, Iccara and Bolfae, who said they'd come from the previously unknown Arach Enclave. Matron Arolina ordered Du'Quelve to secretly transport the two priestesses to her. However, a mission headed by Beniago and Zaknafein Do'Urden was following them. Iccara promised to Du'Quelve a load of arandur and the service of a deep gnome slave, Symvyn Rivenstone. Some time later, Du'Quelve went with Iccara to the meeting with the deep gnome and forced him to dispel the wards of the gnome's outpost. However, Zaknafein, Jarlaxle, and Kimmuriel Oblodra suddenly arrived and in the fighting Iccara was revealed to be a yochlol. In the end, Zaknafein destroyed Iccara, injured Du'Quelve and freed Symvyn.

In later years, Du'Quelve's career stagnated despite her claim to having been in league with two yochlols, as all knew she had only been tricked by the handmaidens.

## Durwyn

Durwyn was a young city guardsman from Phlan who was sucked into the life of adventure when he joined the Veiled Ones in their quest to stop the Cult of the Dragon in Myth Drannor in 1369 DR.

Description
Durwyn was a giant among his peers.  tall, his shoulders and limbs were bulging with muscles. His coarse blond hair was kept short to keep it out of the eyes. His wide face has squared with the jaw that sat atop a thick and muscular neck. Many noticed his kind eyes that turned into fierce orbs of determination in battle.

Personality
Big, strong, and obedient, Durwyn was a loyal man who held his friends' and family's safety as the top priority. He was always passed with promotions due to his lack of leadership skills and personality. It was something Durwyn was content with before his adventures with the Veiled Ones in Myth Drannor.

Durwyn was not a quick thinker, especially in times of crisis. But if discussions about strategies and plans stretched into long tirades, Durwy was ready to suggest a quick and simple approach others tended to overlook.

It usually took a bit of time for others to appreciate Durwyn for his personality rather than for his brawn. He had a big heart, and he always tried to do the right thing even when the right thing was unclear. When his adventuring party argued or was torn by internal conflicts, Durwyn found himself torn between trying to follow each opinion that sounded convincing to him.

Abilities
A trained warrior, Durwyn was proficient with most types of arms and armor. His weapons of choice were battleaxes, light crossbows, and shortbows.

Possessions
Durwyn wore the enchanted +3 chainmail gifted to him by Harldain lronbar of Myth Drannor and wielded a simple battleaxe. Durwyn also carried three bronze-tipped arrows decorated with strange carvings.

Relationships
Durwyn held his abundant family members in high regard and was ready to do everything he could to support them and keep them safe from the dangers of the world.

Ghleanna Stormlake was warm towards Durwyn, thinking him to be a gentle giant. In turn, Durwyn was impressed and smitten by the young half-elf's arcane powers. Corran D'Arcey was an inspiration to Durwyn for the paladin's commanding presence and military skill. Kestrel intimidated the young guardsman with her attitude and sharp tongue, but he grew to respect her mind and admire her keen senses and wits.

History
Durwyn was born on a farm just outside of Phlan, the eighth child out of eleven. The simple life of the household was filled with love, affection, and laughter. His happy childhood was punctuated by often scarcity of food and good clothes, leaving Durwyn and his siblings to wear hand-me-downs from their older kin.

Circa 1364 DR, at the age of thirteen, Durwyn followed his older brother's example and left the family farm to ease the financial strain on them. Just like his brother, he joined Phlan's city guard. Even though he was extremely young, Durwyn was eagerly accepted into the guard, thanks to the kid's size and musculature. With the guards of Phlan, he received his weapons and warrior training, especially excelling in the use of battleaxes and shortbows.

By 1369 DR, Durwyn's been with the guard for five years and was well-respected by his superiors for his loyalty and discipline. He was renowned for unwaveringly and unquestioningly carrying out orders and gladly taking on difficult and taxing tasks. Despite all the accolades, Durwyn was never considered for promotion as the captains fully realized that the burly young man was not a leader. Throughout his service as a guard, he sent half of his pay to his parents weekly.

In 1369 DR, the city of Phlan found its dreaded pool of radiance resurgent in the same chamber it was back when the town was under the tyranny of Tyranthraxus around thirty years prior. Durwyn was tasked with guarding the dangerous life-devouring corrupted pool. Aware of legends and stories of the pools of radiance that dotted Phlan history, Durwyn eagerly took on the task, dedicated to keeping the corruption away from the citizens of the city and especially from his family.

## Dunatis

Dunatis was a minor interloper deity of mountains and peaks who originated from another plane of existence.Dragon #65, "Tuatha De Danann — A revised Celtic mythos" notes that the Deities & Demigods sourcebook's source for Dunatis is a mystery. But the author of that article does note that Welsh versions of the Mabinogion briefly mention someone named Ossol, quoting from it, “under whose feet the highest mountain straightaway became a plain.”

Description
The avatar form of Dunatis was that of a rather ordinary looking human male.

Personality
Dunatis was allegedly one of the most approachable members of his pantheon, as he found himself rarely called upon due to his portfolio. He was even known to prefer spreading the word of his faith himself.

Abilities
Dunatis was immune to charm, paralysis, and petrification effects.

The most notable power of Dunatis was to flatten large mountains into flat plains and vice versa. This effect occurred instantaneously and was often utilized to destroy an enemy's fortifications. He could also manifest boulders in thin air and throw them with great accuracy at targets up to  away.

Possessions
Dunatis owned a shield and set of armor, both with a +4 enchantment, that he always donned when entering combat.

History
In the kingdom of Bakar, a survivor state of the Imaskar Empire, a mighty city known as Medinat Muskawoon housed temples to gods of faiths that the populace's ancestors brought over from their original worlds. Among these temples was one dedicated to Dunatis.

Realm

Dunatis lived within the Outlands, in the divine realm of Tir na Og. His own divine realm within Tir na Og was a vast mountain on its fringe, known as the Pinnacle. Though just a single mountain, the Pinnacle's terrain varied considerably across its surface, from nearly flat areas to sheer, steep rocks. Trees covered the slopes of this mountain and wild animals could be found roaming freely about it.

Worshipers
Dunatis was known to be revered by some rilmani.

Rumors & Legends
This deity claimed to have been the one responsible for the creation of the Spire that stood at the center of the Outlands.

## Durdyn's Devils

Durdyn's Devils were a small mercenary band, mainly composed of drow adventurers. As of, they were investigating Sonjar's Tower for information on the gods of Faerûn on behalf of House Vrasl, while hoping to uncover some Imaskari secret on how to thwart the gods. House Vrasl thought such secrets could be used to uncover the reason for Lolth's silence.

Members
Durdyn was a drow fighter of great skill who had adapted himself to sunlight. He wielded an anarchic greatsword.

Ewa was a tiefling monk and a Thayan refugee, who had been initiated as a monk of the Long Death. She had grown wings through her bloodline.

Rayeth á el Kapyn was a half-drow fighter from the streets of Undrek'Thoz. He had impressed Durdyn with his exceptional fighting skills and also his open disdain of the drow matriarchy. He fought with a spiked chain.

Arrak Abaeir was a rogue, and Durdyn's right hand. He favored the use of a crossbow tipped with poison bolts.

Xi'an and Karushon were siblings assigned to monitor Durdyn's activities. Xi'an was an arachnomancer, against her family's wishes; she had positioned herself to seize control of House Vrasl upon her return. Karushon was a senior mage of the same house.

Others
A young adult dragon, Shianax, was acting as their guide; her lair was a day's walk from the geode, in the Raurin desert.

## Dust of appearance

Dust of appearance was an enchanted powder capable of revealing invisible objects.

Powers
When thrown into the air, this fine metallic powder would coat everything, revealing all hidden objects, including those that were invisible, out of phase, ethereal, etc. The dust also revealed mirror images and projected images for their true nature. The effects of items such as a cloak of displacement or robe of blending were negated as well. The dust worked to reveal both living and nonliving things, lasting for 2–20 minutes.

Dust of appearance was stored in hollow bone blow tubes within a pouch or in small packets made of silk. Each tube represented a single use, covering a  radius when thrown. The dust would cover a cone area measuring  at the base by  long and  wide at the end if blown from the tube.

Notable Owners
Noora bint Amon, a beloved hakima from Zakhara, carried some dust of appearance.

## Duhlark's animerge

Duhlark's animerge was an arcane alteration spell that combined two different creatures into one being. The resultant creature was considered a form of duhlarkin.

Effects
This spell fused two different creatures into a single unique form, combining traits of both into the new form. This spell would not function, and often killed, creatures that possessed any magical abilities. Transmuters who cast this spell could "design" the combined creature to their liking, but the results were random when the spell was cast by any other kind of wizard.

Both subject creatures needed to be within  of each other when the spell was cast and the new creature would appear where the larger creature was standing. There was a good chance that the magic of the spell would kill one or both creatures, resulting in the spell's failure, but the spell would function properly if both creatures survived the casting. There was a higher chance that the spell would fail if one of the creatures was warm-blooded and the other was cold-blooded. Creatures possessing intelligence higher than that of an animal were likely to have their mind shattered during the casting, resulting in an uncontrollable wild creation. The resulting creature was likely not able to breed successfully unless the animerge was sealed with a permanency spell.

The new creature was a permanent creation unless undone by the reverse of this spell, Duhlark's separation.

Components
In addition to verbal and somatic components, this spell required as material components the two target animals to cast.

History
This spell was developed by Duhlark Kolat, a powerful mage from Waterdeep. Wicked spellcasters used this spell to create broken ones. Duhlark himself was known to have made four wolveravens with the spell.

A copy of Duhlark's animerge was stolen, amongst other magics and secrets, from Kolat Towers on the orders of Hlanta Melshimber before being fenced to an apparently reputable dealer. Under a new name, join animal, this spell was sold to Nestor Podgin in the  and would become a key part of his business, Monsters Made to Order, and the creation of the russet owlmonster.

Appendix
References

---

## Duhlark's glasstrike trap

Duhlark's glasstrike trap was an arcane alteration spell that was a variation of Duhlark's glasstrike cast upon glass surface.

Effects
This spell created a magical trap when cast upon a glass surface. Once cast the trap remained active for several days depending on the skill of the caster. The glasstrike tap would trigger when a creature came within  of the glass surface, activating the glasstrike spell. The caster could chose specific parameters that would trigger the trap. For example, the trap could be set to trigger only when touched by a humanoid to avoid being accidentally set off by wildlife such as squirrels.

The glasstrike trap could be counted by Duhlark's glassremove.

Components
In addition to verbal and somatic components, this spell required as material components some sand or a small piece of glass to cast.

History
This spell was developed by Duhlark Kolat, a powerful mage from Waterdeep.

Appendix
References

---

## Duhlark's long reach

Duhlark's long reach was an arcane alteration and conjuration spell that created a magical arm under the caster's control.

Effects
The arm created by this spell was formed out of any material present at the casting location and could be the size of a regular human arm or giant sized depending on the skill of the caster. However, once formed the arm was stationary and its size could not be changed.

The caster could direct the arm to hold objects or creatures, punch enemies, knock down walls, etc. The caster could even use the arm to deliver touch-based spells to a target. The durability of the arm depended on the material it was composed of. The arm would collapse into a pile of its raw materials if destroyed.

Components
In addition to verbal and somatic components, this spell required as material components a small piece of wood or stone and a pinch of sulphur to cast.

History
This spell was developed by Duhlark Kolat, a powerful mage from Waterdeep. Watch wizards of the Watch of Waterdeep were fond of using this spell to apprehend criminals, dubbing it the "Long Arm of the Law."

Appendix
References

---

## Duplicate

Duplicate was a divine alteration and evocation spell used by priests devoted to Oghma, enabling them to copy written inscriptions.

Effects
This spell created a magical quill that appeared in midair. The quill would copy any writing or inscription of the caster's choosing onto a suitable writing surface. This spell was useful for copying other spells and scrolls. The quill would not trigger any magical traps or runes present, but would transfer the same traps onto the newly copied item. The copied writing would appear identical to the original, making this spell useful for forgeries as well.

The caster could only manage one duplicate at a time and attempting to copy more inscriptions would result in feeblemindedness and the loss of the caster's mental acuity to the point where they could actually die. Casters who perished in this manner lost all spellcasting abilities if raised or resurrected.

Components
In addition to verbal and somatic components, this spell required as a material component a feather to cast.

Appendix
References

---

## Durlan

Durlan was a moon elven wizard inhabiting Selgaunt in the mid-to-late 14 century DR.

Activities
Durlan was considered a good and noble person. He spent his time working for the good of all beings.

Hisotry
Some years before the, Durlan met Thazienne Uskevren, a daughter of the Uskevren family of Selgaunt. The noble's daughter spent her childhood on the unsavory streets where she mastered her thieves's skills. The moon elf was found of the child, and after she saved a baby from the midden, Durlan gifted her an emerald ring of projection that one day saved Thazi's life.

## Dung

Dung was a waste product commonly used as fuel for cooking and heating in the Realms. The word in Common was naeth.

Heaps of dung were sometimes found within dungeons, and bat dung, known as guano, was actively mined in many lands in Faerûn.

Properties
Alchemists claimed that the dung of certain dragons had many special properties, such as quick ignition even in cold or wet conditions and the production of an intense blue flame. A red dragon's dung contained significant quantities of potassium nitrate and sulfur, making it potentially explosive, as well as metal and stone stomach stones.

Applications
In desert regions, dried camel dung (called jalla in Midani) was often used as fuel; in the cold reaches of the far north, the dung of local pack animals was used instead, such as that of the rothé. Some farmers used the dung of pack animals as fertilizer, including donkeys, horses, and mules.

Dung was a common material component of several spells. A pinch of bull's dung was used as one of the possible components required for the arcane version of the bull's strength spell and its derivations. Similarly, fox dung was potentially used when casting the spell fox's cunning, and eagle's droppings were used for eagle's splendor. (In all cases, hair or feathers from the respective animals could be used as an alternative material component.) Bat guano was combined with sulfur into a tiny ball that was used in the casting of the spell fireball.

Dragon's dung was smeared on wounds as a medicine to quicken growth and healing.

Reputation
Words for dung in various languages were used as curses. Naeth was considered mild, but when pronounced naed, the word was considered stronger language. In the Tymantheran dialect of Draconic, one of the words for dung, aithyas, was considered an expletive.

Removal

Sewers were used to carry away dung from many cities, but in places without access to streams or where tidal changes were problematic, dung wagons were used instead. In the cities of Suzail and Athkatla, which did not have well-working sewer systems, the dung from people's chamber pots carried off by such wagons was called "'night soil'". Dung wagons were often used by smugglers to transport items or even people out of such cities. The job of carting away dung was often given to the disabled, poor, orphans, or others claiming sanctuary at various temples or monasteries.

A class of creatures known as gulguthras were known to consume dung as part of their diet.

In Sumbria, one of the city-states of the Blade Kingdoms, daily offerings of all the town's "seats of ease" and outhouses were collected by honey carts every evening. The manure was carted to the shores of Akanamere and deposited onto a honey barge. From there, the odoriferous vessel traveled to distant farms on Akanamere shores, blessing them with quality fertilizer.

Variations
The dung produced by cows was referred to as cow chips by inhabitants of Waterdeep and the Delimbiyr Vale. The cow dung scent was similar to the smell of Modovian dung beetles.

Mephit guano was a substance with some magical properties. It was used to enchant items and, if thrown, exploded in a deadly stinking cloud. Carrying such items around was frowned upon and their stench was surely to scare people away.

Trivia
 One of the entrances to the city of Zazesspur was called the Dung Gate.
 Personal hygiene in regards of cleaning nightsoil off varied. Some used hand-scoop method, travelers could use sand along a river bank, others "skidded their bum along in the snow," the wealthy were known to use scented thareea wipe cloths, as well as tough leaves of the thallow and yahllavur-fern plants.

## Durto Bonebreaker

Durto Bonebreaker was a dwarven butcher in the city of Ravens Bluff, who operated with his sibling the Two Brothers' Butchery in the 14 century DR.

Description
Durto wore his hair in three braids and was missing his right ear. He was stockier than most dwarves, standing  tall and weighing around .

Personality
Durto had a very stern and reserved demeanor, especially to strangers. He much preferred to do business with those he already, treating newcomers or adventurers in a blunt manner. Durto was also a workaholic, being happiest when he was busy butchering or sawing ice, but valued gold most of all. Though as far as his wife knew, she was what mattered most to him.

Abilities
Durto was proficient in fighting with battleaxes, handaxes, short swords, and spears.
Possessions
He possessed a battleaxe +3, a set of bracers of defense, and a ring of spell storing. The latter was on loan to him by Rufus Railsplitter, the owner of the Two Brothers' Butchery, and it was loaded with the spell wyvern watch.

Activities
Once a week, when he was not working at the Two Brothers' Butchery, Durto spent his time at the local Dancing Bear Inn. He usually went with his wife and drank excessively.

History
Durto was born to the Bonebreaker family and was a brother of Norin Bonebreaker.

In his youth Durto lived a dangerous life, at some point marrying a dwarf by the name of Naia, until one day during a fight he lost his right ear. Soon after that, Durto took up the profession of a butcher.

## Dundar

Dundar was a blacksmith and inhabitant of Tilverton in the mid-to-late 14 century DR.

Activities
Dundar owned Dundar's Fine Swords where he in fact, sold fine swords. By the late 1360's he had expanded his craft to include saws, axes, scythes, and other bladed implements, and renamed his shop to Dundar's Fine Blades. He also provided training for novice fighters in weaponplay.

Relationships
Dundar's sibling, a Harper named Andalara, helped him in the shop by finishing blades and constructing scabbards when she wasn't hunting.

## Dundar's Fine Swords

Dundar's Fine Swords was a weapon shop that specialized in swords located in Tilverton in the .

Location
The shop sat in the south-eastern part of Tilverton on a side street off the Street of the Sorceress.

Inhabitants
The shop was owned by a retired fighter named Dundar. His sister Andalara helped finish the blades and make scabbards when she was not out hunting.

Services
Around 1366 DR Dundar had expanded his craft and started selling axes, scythes, saws, and other bladed implements, eventually changing the shops name to Dundar's Fine Blades. He was sometimes assisted by his sibling Andalara, who finished weapons and made scabbards. Dundar also provided weapons training for aspiring warriors, drawing on his own significant skill and experience.

## Dung beetle

Dung beetles were various species of mundane beetles whose names were often used as insults. However, the scarab dung beetles were respected and their likeness even was used to craft various enchanted amulets.

Description
Dung beetles, like other beetles, had a tough external carapace and were known for their virile breeding.

Ecology
Habitats
A species of Modovian dung beetle‎s was known to be found in Waterdeep and was a delicacy among local lizards.

Sembian farmers used to "drain" the land of all nourishment before they learned to rotate different types of crops grown. Areas that were damaged by this thoughtless farming became only suitable to sustain dung beetles and ragweed.

History
After Volothamp Geddarm's work, Volo's Guide to All Things Magical, was published circa 1369 DR, he angered a great number of wizards. Snilloc threatened Volo with spending the eternity as a dung beetle.

Sometime before 1372 DR, Purfbin Doogrick, a rogueish adventurer from Waterdeep was stuck in Lady Spitalsmore's basement, as the noblewoman came back home earlier than anticipated. The ingenious gnome used a handful of Modovian dung beetle‎s to fashion a trap for lizards and then used lizards to scare the old woman out of her home, allowing the little thief to escape with a handful of food from the Waterdhavian noble's pantry.

## Duergahydra

Duergahydras, also known as the gray evil of the Underdark, were an extinct species of hydra that were notably quite smaller than other subspecies of hydra and inhabited the Underdark.

Description
These creatures had a hard, gray stony hide on what some would describe as a whip-like build. They had a central body the size of a tall human, two long necks each sporting a head, and a long tail. They typically kept one of their two heads bent around their body, laying hidden on their back to resemble a hump or folded wing.

Behavior
These creatures were quite aggressive and bold in their hunting, frequently sticking their necks out into danger, owing to instinctively being aware of their regenerative ability. They were also quite cunning, quickly learning the details and tactics of any foe they faced.

Abilities
Duergahydras were swift and agile climbers, capable of clinging to walls, and could see in areas of complete darkness. Like any hydra, they had a regenerative ability that would grow two necks and heads whenever one was severed. However, due to their size, they became slower with each head and neck they gained until they were reduced to a crawling movement and eventually became too slow to hunt and thus starved.

Ecology
Duergahydras were a rather solitary species, only pairing up when it was necessary to mate.

Diet
These creatures were an omnivorous species.

Usages
The blood of these creatures was quite useful for the creation of healing potions and regenerative potions.

History
The useful applications of the duergahydras' blood was discovered by drow and mindflayers, who hunted the creatures into near-extinction in an effort to harvest their ichor. Later on, humans became aware of their existence and attempted to train them as guardians or mounts. When this and efforts to keep duergahydras away from human settlements both proved impossible, humans began to slaughter the creatures whenever they saw one. Eventually, duergahydras went extinct on Toril around the year 50 DR.

## Durang

The Durang were a tribe of raiding ogres somewhere in Faerûn in the mid–14 century DR.

Activities
The Durang were a raiding tribe.

Possessions
The leader of the Durang wore a shirt made of flattened human skins, the faces of which were animated and capable of biting. He also bore a two-handed club,  in length, reinforced with both iron nails and the teeth of many beasts.

History
When Marrec joined the town militia at the age of sixteen, he fought the Durang in a raid on the village proper. Though they slew his adoptive brother Emmon, Marrec discovered his petrifying medusa gaze while fighting; they retreated after seeing it in action. They made a second raid some time after; though Marrec had been banished from the village, he came to its aid together with his mentor, Thanial Selwander. The Durang leader personally led the raid this time around; the young man was forced to resort to his medusa gaze when his master was caught by the ogre chief. Though the ogre was petrified and his tribesmen retreated, Thanial Selwander was affected by the gaze too; after both of them fell over, their stone forms were shattered beyond recovery.

Notable Members
The Durang leader was an ogre about twice the size of his comrades, who could speak Common. He spoke with a foul, phlegmy tongue.

## Dunnol

Dunnol was a duergar mind master that joined the cult of the Absolute during the late 15 century DR.

Personality
He was a brusque man that was wholly dedicated to his work for the cult.

Abilities
Like other dwarven mind masters, Dunnol wielded psionic powers that could briefly take over the minds of others, causing them to attack friends or allies in battle.

Possessions
Dunnol wore padded clothes and covered his shoulders with a white cape, along with the distinct metal mask worn by mind master dwarves. He carried with him a lashing rod, that he used to punish laboring slaves.

Activities
Under the supervision of True Soul Nere, Dunnol served as a taskmaster for the cult of the Absolute. He chided and beat the deep gnome slaves they used to excavate long-lost ruins within the Underdark.

History
In the, Dunnol was among the Absolute's novices that journeyed into the ruins of Grymforge in search of an old temple of Shar that remained within its depths.

## Duhlarkin

A duhlarkin was any creature created through the use of the Duhlark's animerge spell.

Description
Since Duhlark's animerge allowed the caster to fuse two different creatures into one new creature that possessed physical traits of both, the description of a duhlarkin varied greatly.

Combat
The type of physical attacks that a duhlarkin was capable of depended on the physical attributes it acquired from its "parent" creatures.

Society
The general behavior of a duhlarkin was similar to that of the dominant creature used during its creation. A duhlarkin was capable of reproducing by mating with either of the creatures it was related to. The offspring from these unions were stillborn a quarter of the time, resulted in a duhlarkin a quarter of the time, and resulted in the pure creature half of the time. A pair of mating duhlarkins would result in a stillborn half of the time and duhlarkin half of the time.

## Dungeon magazine 159/Menace of the Icy Spire

"Menace of the Icy Spire" is a short adventure for Dungeons & Dragons 4 edition published in Dungeon #159.

Synopsis
In Eleasis of 1479 DR, a group of adventurers is hired to investigate the unseasonably cold weather in the Gray Vale. Local leaders in Loudwater suspect this is a magical phenomenon emanating from the tower of the dwarven warlock Draigdurroch, who disappeared thirty years ago. The adventurers must investigate Draigdurroch Tower, overcoming frost goblins, ice warriors, and a spirit of winter itself along the way.

## Dungeon magazine 29/Ex Libris

"Ex Libris" is a short adventure for Dungeons & Dragons 2 edition published in Dungeon #29.

Synopsis
The party are hired by the Vault of the Sages to recover any and all books of value from the Bard Keep, a ruined Oghmanyte library-temple located in the Nether Mountains north and east of Silverymoon. What they're not told however, is that the ruins have been declared off-limits by the Church of Oghma.

The priesthood of the Halls of Inspiration therefore, knowing that adventurers have been hired to go to Bard Keep, hire their own group of adventurers to keep the player party from their destination. Additionally, the ruins themselves are haunted by the priests who died during Bard Keep's destruction, and the library room specifically is magical and constantly rearranges itself to trap invaders and prevent them from accessing certain areas.

Should the party overcome their opponents, and learn the secrets of the shifting library, they can return to the Vault of the Sages with a wealth of books and be richly rewarded, but they will have made an enemy of the local Oghmanyte priests.

 The opening of this adventure mentions that it could act as a sequel to "The Oracle at Sumbar", from Dungeon #48.
 The last page of the adventure features an in-character sidebar advertising Of Ships and the Sea.

## Durn Stoneborn

Durn Stoneborn was a passenger aboard the Vigilant in 1374 DR.

Description
Durn was a solemn, heavily armed, and armored duergar. He was not keen on conversing and uttered not a word during the voyage on the Vigilant. Durn used his trusty longsword in battle.

History
In the, Durn Stoneborn was among the passengers aboard Captain Lastri Kassireh's Vigilant that traveled from the Sword Coast to the nation of Samarach on the Chultan Peninsula. Several passengers were onboard the ship, including renowned writer Volothamp Geddarm. The voyage was cursed with bad luck and culminated in a deadly storm. The Vigilant's shipwreck was washed ashore on Omgar's Teeth, not far from the city of Samargol. Durn was among several survivors of the crash and faced a scavenging band of Batiri goblins on the beach shortly after the disaster.

Appendix
Background
 According to Wolfgang Baur, this adventure was inspired by the John Keats poem Ode on Melancholy.
 Notably, this adventure begins by recommending one own the sourcebooks The Savage Frontier and Waterdeep and the North.
 This adventure does not begin in a set location. Rather, it suggests beginning in any metropolis in northwest Faerûn. Giving Neverwinter, Silverymoon, and Waterdeep as examples.
 The wand of petrification in this adventure was undescribed, only later being detailed in the Letters Column of Dungeon #22.

## Dungeons of Despair

Dungeons of Despair is an adventure anthology for 2-edition Dungeons & Dragons featuring six short adventures reprinted from issues of Dungeon. Only one adventure, "Masqueraider", is set in the Forgotten Realms. The anthology is a sequel of sorts to Road to Danger, an earlier collection with the same premise.

Contents
 "Caermor" by Nigel D. Findley, originally printed in Dungeon #2.
 "The Moor-Tomb Map" by Jon Bailey, originally printed in Dungeon #13.
 "The Cauldron of Plenty" by Willie Walsh, originally printed in Dungeon #21.
 "King Oleg's Dilemma" by Lee Shepherd, originally printed in Dungeon #43.
 "Encounter in the Wildwood" by Willie Walsh, originally printed in Dungeon #19.
 "Masqueraider" by Randy Maxwell, originally printed in Dungeon #14.

---

## Durneth Seafarer

High Radiance Durneth Seafarer was the leader of the temple known the Tower of the Morning.

Activities
Durneth was involved in recruiting leading clerics along with Daelegoth Orndeir.

History
Seafarer led the clerics of the Tower of the Morning from at least 1369 to 1374 DR. In, Durneth proclaimed edicts that gave credit to the Risen Sun heresy.

References

## Dumathoin's rest

Dumathoin's rest was a spell used by clerics of Dumathoin.

Effects
When cast, any skeletons and zombies within  collapsed. Any attempts to reanimated them failed for at least a day.

Components
In addition to verbal and somatic components, this spell required a material components, the holy symbol of Dumathoin.

Appendix
References

## Duckweed

Duckweed were fine-leafed flowering aquatic plant found in hot sweltering Chultan climate.

Usages
Duckweed was a part of gas spores' diet. The bloated creatures used their rhizome growths to suck in molds, lichens, pond scum, and duckweed.

Availability
Duckweed could be found growing in the Lake Grunglung in Chult, along with other aquatic plants such as lily pads, and surrounded by lazy catfish.

## Duke's Way

Duke's Way was a road found in the city of Daggerford in the Western Heartlands in the mid-to-late 14 century DR.

Location
Duke's Way began at the Caravan Gate entering Daggerford from the west. It continued east through the center of the city before terminating at the Ducal Castle which covered most of eastern Daggerford.

Notable Locations
The Hill Road intersected Duke's Way at the base of the landrise the separated the ducal estate from the rest of Daggerford, while the High Road branched south from the Hill Road near the drill field. Notable buildings located along Duke's Way included:

 The Ducal Castle
 Daggerford Inn
 The Grand Playhouse
 Kryptgarden Scrolls
 Morninglow Tower
 Oryv's Cloth Emporium

Appendix
Appearances
Gamebooks
Spawn of Dragonspear

## Dungeon magazine 30/...And A Dozen Eggs

"...And A Dozen Eggs" is a Waterdeep adventure for Dungeons & Dragons 2 edition published in Dungeon #30.

Synopsis
Sometime around the month of Eleint, twelve tanystropheus eggs from Chult are purchased by Phalantar's Philtres & Components for use as potion ingredients. However, the eggs hatch overnight in its warm laboratory and the creatures escape into the Waterdhavian sewers through its garbage chute. Phalantar Orivan reports this to the City Watch as soon as he finds out, but that night the young dinosaurs attack members of the Cellarers' & Plumbers' Guild, killing one and leaving two badly mauled. The next day Phalantar puts out a reward of 500 gold pieces per living dinosaur and 100 gold pieces per dead, while the Guild offers an additional reward of 200 gold pieces. A large number of adventurers and bounty hunters in Waterdeep jump at this opportunity.

In an area beneath a large laundry, one group discovers that its warm temperature and regular pouring of soapscum had made the area a breeding ground for fungi, including a collection of shriekers and violet fungi. Amid the fungi, they spot the corpse of a tanystropheus that was chasing a giant rat. After making their way past, the adventurers find a group of bounty hunters struggling to fend off one of the tanystropheuses and jump to their aid.

Later, while at a festhall or tavern, the adventurers are approached by a strange old sailor named Pravus Muridae, who informs them that he knows where one of the dinosaurs are in the sewers and will lead them there in return for splitting the bounty. Leading them into the sewers, Pravus eventually reveals himself to be a cleric of the rat god, who has been using the dinosaurs to lure people into the sewers for the purposes of robbing, feeding his wererat minions, and making sacrifices to his god. Parvus then sicks a group of wererats upon them. He and the rest of his wererat allies potentially escape deeper into the sewers to cause further mischief another day, while some of the tanystropheuses are potentially left unrecovered. However, the latter are presumed lethargic from the cold weather of Marpenoth, then dead from the freezing weather of Uktar, and by Uktar 30 all bounties are rescinded.

## Dusk Street

Dusk Street was a road located in the city of Zhentil Keep during the mid–14 century DR.

Location
Dusk Street was located in Zhentil Keep's Common Quarter in the eastern section of the city. Originating along Border Street at the outer wall of the city, Dusk Street passed southwest for several blocks until terminating at the barrier to the Inner Quarter. Dusk Street was intersected by Beholder Street, Black Street, and Star Street.

Notable Locations
A Zhentilar guardpost was located along Dusk Street where it connected with Border Street at the outer city wall.

## Dualdon's Wake Mead

Dualdon's Wake Mead was an alcoholic drink brewed in the Ten-Towns of Icewind Dale.

Availability
The libation was sold as early as the, in the towns surrounding lake Maer Dualdon. The mead was also served at The Salty Dog tavern in Targos where it was one of the cheaper intoxicants available. Sold for four gold pieces at the bar by its portly proprietor Gohar who was happy to share the town's latest rumors with paying customers.

## Dukar Krabbellor

Dukar Krabbellor, who was nicknamed Hammerhand, was a laborer inhabiting the city of Waterdeep during the mid-to-late 14 century DR.

Activities
Dukar never mentioned his first name to anyone to the point where he nearly forgot it himself. Instead, he prefered to go by Hammerhand. A suiting nickname considering that one of his hands was made from iron. He was obsessed with finding the person responsible for killing his brother in Cormyr. All he knew for certain was that they were a wizard who had a half-orc bodyguard.

Duir's Alley was a small alley that was located in the city of Waterdeep.

Duir's Alley was located in the eastern section of Waterdeep's Castle Ward. It connected the Street of Bells in the west with the Street of the Sword in the east via passing through a neighborhood of buildings. Lhoril's Alley branched off of the southern side of Duir's Alley.

Activities
Patrons of the Elfstone Tavern often exited the building's rear onto Duir's Alley where they displayed their magical prowess.

## Durral

Durral was a half-orc who worked as a guard and bouncer at the Knight 'n' Shadow tavern in Waterdeep as of the .

Description
He was a brute, and his most distinguishing features were his spellscarred hands.

Abilities
Durral's spellscar augmented his fists with magical force, allowing him to throw powerful punches that could instantly knock his opponents off their feet. With some effort, he was able to deliver such a thunderous blow that the force of it could knock down multiple people at once.

Activities
As a bouncer, Durral was responsible for subduing bothersome patrons or intruders—nonlethally if possible—and punishing or locking up troublemakers as needed. Additionally, he and a group of seven human guards protected the  pit that led between the tavern's lower level (called "Shadow") and Downshadow in Undermountain.

Durral was personally responsible for manning the rope-operated lift that lowered folk into Downshadow. He charged 5 gold dragons per person per trip—which was not included in the 25 gold bribe that most visitors to Downshadow had to pay just to reach him from the "Knight" section of the tavern upstairs—and those who didn't give him a generous tip could expect a bumpy ride. He strictly disallowed anyone from attempting to climb down for free.

Although it was not widely known, Durral also helped to smuggle people out of Downshadow.

Relationships
Durral had caught and locked-up one particular gnome pickpocket so frequently that the thief came to be known by the nickname "Stockade".

## Dungeons & Dragons Starter Set (2022)

The Dungeons & Dragons Starter Set is a boxed set for the 5 edition of Dungeons & Dragons. Intended to serve as a basic introduction to tabletop D&D, it contains a set of basic rules, an adventure, several pre-made character sheets, and a set of dice. The adventure is Dragons of Stormwreck Isle, an introductory adventure module for 1–5 player-characters taking them from 1 level to 3 as they explore a mysterious new island.

Contents
 Starter Set Rulebook, a 32-page booklet containing a basic set of rules for adventurers levels 1–3.
 Dragons of Stormwreck Isle, a 48-page adventure booklet
 5 pregenerated character sheets
 6 game dice

## Dungeons & Dragons Starter Set (2022)/Dragons of Stormwreck Isle

Dragons of Stormwreck Isle is a 5th edition introductory adventure included in the Dungeons & Dragons Starter Set (2022) boxed set. It is designed for 1–5 player-characters, taking them from 1 level to 3 as they explore the eponymous Stormwreck Isle, a new location within the Forgotten Realms.

To a greater degree than previous adventures intended as entry points for 5th edition Dungeons & Dragons, such as those included in the original Dungeons & Dragons Starter Set and in the Dungeons & Dragons Essentials Kit, Dragons of Stormwreck Isle is intended primarily to teach how to play the game of Dungeons & Dragons. This means that the adventure booklet contains many more tips and notes for the DM about how to run certain scenes or encounters, how to treat players, and how to engage players. The adventure is intended to be played with the pre-generated characters included in the boxed set, and includes motivations and hooks specific to backgrounds included on those character sheets.

The walled compound contained three buildings and a large courtyard. The buildings were three or four stories tall.

## Duilya Evendusk

Duilya Evendusk was an inhabitant of Cormanthor in the 3 century DR.

Abilities
At one point, she and a number of her friends employed a mind-linking spell and another to convert tripleshroom sherry to water as it was drank.

Description
She was slim, but not as slim as she'd been in her youth.

Possessions
She owned a bower.

Personality
Until, she was fearful of facing her lord. She was, however, sadistic enough to hope that the human armathor's hunt was something they could scry on. She turned out to be reasonable enough to help the others attempt to stabilize Cormanthor in light of the human armathor's situation, however.

Relationships
She had a number of friends whom she let in on every intimate detail. She had grown somewhat distant from her husband in, but they grew closer on that year.

History
In 241, after Elminster Aumar was named Armathor by Coronal Eltargrim Irithyl, she met with the ladies of other houses at Satyrdance Pool. There, while she was rebuked by her friends for her indifference to the fate of Cormanthor, she ultimately agreed to get closer to her own husband to help stabilize the city, as the human armathor was expected to be a prelude to a new Coronal and thus a period of strife. Later, using a spell to link her mind to that of her friends, she got her husband's attention.

Later that year, after the future raising of the Mythal was announced, she told her husband that it was a possibility that a future Coronal might use the mythal differently, as there was no reason its spells should not affect elves.

In, Duilya was present at court during the laying of the mythal.

## Duracles

Duracles  was the pit fiend of the Nine Hells that oversaw the domain of Mirror Maelstrom on the plane of Avernus.

Personality
Duracles was derisive towards his lessers and arrogant about his powers in combat. He always picked a fight whenever possible in order to demonstrate his martial prowess to others.

Abilities
He was quite adept at uncovering how best to provoke his victims and draw out their most deep-seated insecurities and fears.

Relationships
To carry out his duties, Duracles worked alongside the mortal Lysander Sallowbone, a Red Wizard zulkir from the world of Toril. As a gesture of good faith, he gifted the wizard an oathbow crafted from hardened lava.

He was accompanied at all times by two kyton minions.

History
Some time during or after the 1480s DR, Duracles was directed by Asmodeus to watch over the imprisonment and torture of the soul of King Hekaton of Faerûn. He spent countless days torturing the storm giant king, until his mind was entirely fractured.

## Dung Gate

The Dung Gate was one of the entrances into the city of Zazesspur in Tethyr.

Description
The Dung Gate was a barbican gate with two portcullises; however, only one of the portcullises was functional in the year 1370 DR. It provided access through the northeastern wall of Zazesspur, which was  high. It was one of the older gates to the city, but being an entrance into the High City, north of the Sulduskoon River, it was not as old as the oldest structures of the city.

Defenses
Like other gates of the city, it was guarded by six troops, who could sound an alarm for reinforcements if needed. The gate was closed at sunset and reopened at sunrise.

History
In the, Zaranda Star and her caravan entered the city through a breach in the wall near the Dung Gate, to avoid having to bribe the guards to enter.

## Durham Shaw

Captain Durham Shaw was a leader among the Neverwinter Guard in the late 15 century DR.

Description
Durham visibly suffered from effects of the Spellplague.

Relationships
Captain Shaw worked for Lord Dagult Neverember and served under General Sabine. Many of his subordinates in the Neverwinter Guard did not respect him.

History
Durham was Captain of the Wall as of the . By and large, the local Neverwintian soldiers who had long manned the Wall despised him and resented taking orders from him. Beyond their normal distaste for New Neverwinter mercenaries, the soldiers believed he was receiving preferential treatment by being allowed to remain in the city despite Neverember's standing edict that all individuals affected by the Spellplague be expelled from Neverwinter.

Rumors & Legends
It was believed that Durham and Lord Dagult Neverember were either personal friends or at least distantly related.

References

## Duric Green

Duric Green  was one of the owners of Green's Rest, an inn and tavern located in the city of Waterdeep during the late 15 century DR.

Description
Duric was a sturdy man sporting an impressive beard under a balding head.

Personality
His husband Zel and his business meant everything to Durin. Often jovial and smiling, he loved to hear entertaining stories from adventurers. He was willing to offer a free meal and drinks to people entertaining him and his guests with tales of their daring deeds.

Duric had a positive outlook on life, and was always ready to offer words of encouragements to guests. He was, however, terrible at keeping secrets.

## Dualdon's Wake

Dualdon's Wake was a small but important cargo ship from the town of Targos in Icewind Dale, active in the early 14 century DR.

Description
Dualdon's Wake was a long two-masted cargo ship with its name proudly inscribed on the hull.

History
Circa the, the Dualdon's Wake sailed the waters of Maer Dualdon and the Shaengarne River. When Targos became besieged by the goblin and orc forces of the Legion of the Chimera in Mirtul of the, Dualdon's Wake was used to transport lumber from Lonelywood to Targos for the construction of defensive palisades. The ship was targeted by goblin arsonists trying to cripple Targos by destroying its docks, but the invaders were slaughtered by a group of adventurers from Luskan, and the ship was defended by a member of the Targos Guard named Crandall.

## Dusk unicorn

A dusk unicorn was a rare breed of unicorn. Some fey believed that the birth of one was an auspicious omen.

Description
Unlike regular unicorns, dusk unicorns possessed a dark coat and mane and a black horn.

Abilities
Dusk unicorns, like other unicorns, could heal other creatures by touching them with their horn. Additionally, a dusk unicorn possessed a further two unusual magical abilities: they could manifest a group of dancing lights that drew other creatures towards the light, and they could teleport themselves or a group of allies up to .

Combat
Dusk unicorns wielded their hooves and their horn as weapons; in particular, charging into an enemy horn-first was an effective tactic of theirs.

## Dungeons & Dragons Annual 2022

Dungeons & Dragons Annual 2022 is a comic published by IDW Publishing in August, 2022, set largely in the Feywild, as a tie-in with The Wild Beyond the Witchlight adventure. It comprised two stories, Lost & Found and Something for Everyone, written by Jim Zub and Ryan Cady, respectively.

Summary
In Lost & Found, a young adventurer joins an unlikely companion on a journey to the Witchlight Carnival and the Feywild beyond, in search for missing memories and lost family.

Something For Everyone sees two star-crossed lovers—a pixie and a sprite on opposite sides of the law—fight for their love despite  the odds stacked against them.

## Dungeon magazine 62/Grimjaws

"Grimjaws" is a short adventure for Dungeons & Dragons 2 edition published in Dungeon #62. Set in the Vast Swamp, the adventure heavily references the book Elminster's Ecologies: The Cormyrean Marshes.

Synopsis
In the mid-14 century DR, a shaman-in-training lizardman from the Tun Marshes by the name of Sutholo moves to the Vast Swamp to study the local floral and fauna. One day he kills a large crocodile near his hut along the Darkflow River and tries to preserve its body by using herbs, wrapping tightly around it in cloth. At night a storm blows the carcass into the Darkflow proper, where evil magic within its waters cause the crocodile to arise from the dead as a mummy. The crocodile goes on to infect creatures in the surrounding area, including Sutholo, with mummy rot and eventually becomes known as Grimjaws.

Later a group of adventurers are drawn towards the area due to tavern gossip about a small cache of tools, etched in Espruar runes, that were retrieved from the swamp. Before long the adventurers encounter Sutholo's hut, where they possibly attempt to heal the lizardman of his mummy rot and try to slay the terrible mummy Grimjaws.

## Dutiocs

Sir Dutiocs was the Chief of the Roadwarden Guard, stationed in the Skull Crag Keep in the Storm Horn Mountains of Cormyr in the 14 century DR.

Description
Sir Dutiocs was an honorable and experienced iron-clad human knight. He was 57 years old in the early 1360s DR.

Possessions
As a knight of the keep, he wielded a +3 enchanted long sword in battle. He wore an enchanted +3 elfin chain, and a cloak of displacement. Among other items in his possession were darts of the hornets' nest, a potion of speed, and a ring of cold resistance.

History
By the early 1360s DR, Sir Dutiocs was already the Chief of the Roadwarden Guard, a Cormyrean order dedicated to patrolling and keeping the High Road safe for merchant caravans that entered and left the Forest Kingdom through the Storm Horns. Dutiocs and his men served under the Roadwarden of the Skull Crag Keep – a renowned hero Arelin Starbrow. In the early 1360s DR, the Roadwarden fell to a monster onslaught leaving Sir Dutiocs galloping away from danger along with her body. As he retreated, he was forced to leave behind the Arms of the Roadwarden, symbols of the office. Sir Dutiocs' steed received an arrow to its hind leg as the knight approached the gates of Skull Crag, blaring his signal horn. Luckily, he was greeted by a group of caravan guards who had recently ended their contract in the village.

The adventurers and Sir Dutiocs stood against a monster band that chased the knight across the mountain. A fierce battle ensured against a collection of orges, hill giants, minotaurs, and an oni mage. Luckily, the battle was won; however, the danger remained. The monster attack was just a harbinger, and a horde soon besieged the mountain road leading towards Skull Crag and collapsed the town's underground passages, cutting it off from any escape routes. The heroes recognized the dead body – it was the local hero Arelin Starbrow. Sir Dutiocs invited his saviors to the Skull Crag Keep, sure that the Roadwarden Guard would soon need help against the horde and aid with the recovery of the Arms of the Roadwarden. He wrote a Writ of Passage for the group and rushed to the keep.

## Dunzrin's harp

Dunzrin's harp was a magical harp used by the Harpers.

Powers
Through a silent act of will, the last person to play Dunzrin's harp could command it to record speech, music, and other sounds, and later play them back upon request. This feature allowed non-harpists to feign playing a beautiful melody while playing back a previously recorded tune. Additionally, a spying Harper could use the instrument to capture an incriminating conversation for others to hear.

The harp had the ability to store up to three different sound recordings, each lasting several minutes. The user could instruct the harp to ignore any music it played during recording, ensuring that musical sounds did not interfere with the recording of other sounds. However, the fourth recording automatically erased the earliest recording, regardless of its length or the user's preference.

## Duskblade

The Duskblade was an enchanted halberd once belonged to a deceased adventurer Patrick at the moment of his death in the late 14 century DR.

Description
The weapon was quote mundane in its appearance, with the metal of the halberd becoming tinted with umbral colors of the night.

Powers
The Duskblade absorbed the dark energies of the night and the deathly cold of darkness that made it a deadly weapon enchanted to freeze its targets upon successful attacks.

History
Duskblade once was an ordinary halberd that became an enchanted weapon after being left by a powerful lich's soul phylactery for several hundreds of years. Some years before the, the lich was destroyed, likely by others of his kin. In 1369 DR, a ranger companion of Mazzy Fentan named Patrick wielded the Duskblade against the Shade Lord and his pet dragon Thaxll'ssillyia, who infested an ancient ruined temple dedicated to Amaunator with darkness. Patrick perished in the struggle, and his undead form, now a shade, wielded the Duskblade against anyone who dared to enter the temple.

## Dungeon magazine 9/The Golden Bowl of Ashu H'San

"The Glass House" is an Oriental Adventures module for Dungeons & Dragons 1 edition, published in Dungeon #9, that takes place somewhere in the land of Kozakura.It is never stated in the adventure which nation the events are taking place in. However, the adventure uses some Japanese terms stated in Swords of the Daimyo to be specific to the Kozakuran language. In particular, hyakusho, a term for peasants, is apparently unique to Kozakura. Therefore, for convenience, this wiki presumes it is set in Kozakura.

## Dust of tracelessness

Dust of tracelessness was an enchanted powder that covered up the tracks of its user and other nearby creatures.

Description
This power was typically found within finely sewn pouches, each containing around 13 to 24 pinches-worth of the substance.

Powers
When a pinch of this powder was into the air within an interior space, it caused a floor space of up to  to take on an appearance of decades of disuse, becoming dusty, dirty, laden in cobwebs. When used in an outdoor space, such as being sprinkled along a trail, a pinch of this powder would cause all evidence of the user and up to a dozen other human-sized individuals and horses to be
erased for as far back as a . Regardless of where it was used, this powder did not produce any dweomer.

Later iterations of this power in the late 14 century DR were comparatively less potent, only affecting a chamber space of up to  and only removing outdoor trails as far back as  into the distance. However, these later iterations continued to leave no lingering aura after use.

Creation
Creating some dust of tracelessness required use of the spell pass without trace.

Notable Owners
 Jannaxil Serpentil, a bookseller in Waterdeep, owned a pouch of dust of tracelessness that he either carried on his person or kept close at hand within his office.
 Rornar Gordrinn, a shield dwarf in the Silver Marches dedicated to slaying orcs, owned a pouch of dust of tracelessness.
 Sigird Sigirdson, a thief in the the Vast, owned a pouch of dust of tracelessness.
 Torm, a member of the Knights of Myth Drannor who was a notorious hoarder of magic devices and trinkets, owned a pouch of dust of tracelessness'' and sometimes kept it on his person.

Appearances

## Dumbwaiter

A dumbwaiter, known in Common as a durthdra, was a type of lift designed for carrying food and small items though some were large enough to carry furniture or even a person.

Description
Dumbwaiters consisted of an open-fronted box that came in a variety of sizes, with some being large enough that five human-sized people could cram together inside of one. They were housed within shafts and raised or lowered between the floors of a building by means of ropes attached to a pulley system.

Etymology
The term "durthdra" was a compound word, made up of the Alzhedo words "durth" and "uldra", meaning "platform" and "rope" or "pulley" respectively. Though made up of Alzhedo words, durthdra was not a proper word within the Alzhedo language and was little known by its speakers as such contraptions were rare in west Faerûn.

Notable Users
Businesses
 The Cutlass, a popular tavern in Luskan, had a large dumbwaiter.
 Halaband's Inn, an establishment in Immersea, had a dumbwaiter that bats were prone to riding upon.
 The Nine Fires, an inn in the city of Arabel, had a dumbwaiter that allowed the staff to quickly deliver warm meals to most rooms.
 The Purple Lady, a festhall in the city of Westgate, had a dumbwaiter that connected the kitchen to a kitchen antechamber within one of the private meeting-rooms on the second floor.
 The Sign of the Shield, an inn in Voonlar, boasted the only dumbwaiter in the small farming town.
 Spath Investigations, a detective firm in Ravens Bluff, had a dumbwaiter.
Private Residences
 Blackstaff Tower had a dumbwaiter.
 Castle Spulzeer had a dumbwaiter that was large enough for four human-sized creatures to stand in, leading up to the banquet facilities on the second floor. It was notably haunted by a weeping spirit.
 Cholandrothipe's Tower had a dumbwaiter connecting all of its levels.
 Fzoul's Tower had a dumbwaiter that connected its feasting hall to the tower's kitchen and cellar.
 Oldspires, an old mansion belonging to Sardasper Halaunt.
 The fortress city of Orcus had two large dumbwaiters that were ridden by dretch servitors.
 Phelhelra, a fortess on the Marching Mountains, had a great deal of small dumbwaiters that connected the various rooms of the complex.
 Raventree Villa, the private estate of House Raventree, had a dumbwaiter whose shaft climbed from the kitchen all the way up to the bedchambers of the villa's tallest tower.

## Duthspurn

Duthspurn was a Zhentilar swordsman in the mid-14 century DR. His legs were recognizable.

Description
He was known to have multiple legs, almost certainly two. His legs were easily recognizable.

Possessions
He was wearing boots prior to his death. Those were not needed to recognize him.

Relationships
He was under the command of Lyrkon, in a squad including the mageling Fimril. The swordmaster Lyrkon could easily recognize him by his legs.

History
In, Duthspurn's squad struck at Shandril Shessair, with intent to capture, in Hullack Forest. As a countermeasure for her spellfire, Fimril used a spell of all-consuming darkness, which generated a slowly advancing dark band ahead of his men. Shandril's spellfire could not penetrate the barrier, but by striking under the band, she managed to disintegrate one of the men's legs; he tripped forward, screaming, and fell into the band of darkness, which annihilated selectively the top half of his body.

Though their capture initially succeeded, it was at the cost of five casualties of their own, including Duthspurn himself, who had been reduced to a pair of legs. This was sufficient for his identification.

## Duskreene

Lady Duskreene was a Netherese inhabitant of Tethgard, and later, a watchghost.

Description
She was a wraithlike apparition, with white, snaking hair. Her form was skeletal below the breasts and indeed legless, resembling a living woman above that line.

Personality
Lady Duskreene was lonely, and friendly.

Abilities
She was somehow capable of slaying people as a watchghost.

Activities
In life, Lady Duskreene ruled a small area around Tethgard. In death, she often watched the area around her old holdings.

Relationships
During her tenure as a watchghost she'd made an acquaintance of a far-removed descendant of hers, Mirt, who often came tell her of the goings-on in the world. She'd also made an acquaintance of a younger Elminster.

History
Prior to the founding of Cormyr, Lady Duskreene, a Netherese, had ruled the lands of Tethgard in the Hullack Forest. She was interred in her family crypt and then rose as a watchghost; during her tenure as an undead over the ruin, she'd made the acquaintance of a younger Elminster, and much later on, of Mirt, who was a remote descendant of her line.

In the late, Mirt guided Delg, Shandril Shessair and Narm Tamaraith to Tethgard as a safety measure, as they were pursued by Zhentilar. While there, Mirt was forced to call out to Duskreene to get her aid in destroying the Zhentilar. She later made an acquaintance of his guests.

## Dunstable's Dreams

Dunstable's Dreams was a renowned language reference book. Politely called Dreams and casually called Dunstable, it was well-respected and much-referred to in the Heartlands of the Realms.

Description
The book was only the size of a human hand, but was extremely thick. A newer edition came with sturdy leather slipcovers with four thong catches, intended to help protect the spine of the book from cracking so swiftly.

Contents
The book collected all sorts of useful and witty mottos, sayings, retorts, turns of phrase, and new words from all across Faerûn, going back to the origins of the Common tongue.

History
Dunstable's Dreams was very popular in the 14 century DR, and regularly received new editions.

## Duergar-forged axe

Duergar-Forged Axes were huge perfectly-balanced enchanted battle axes of dull gray steel, forged and ensorcelled by the duergar dwarves underneath the Spine of the World.

Powers
The sharpness of these dangerous axes was evident that the duergar smiths used magic in their creation. Duergar-forged axes possessed +1 enchantments to the weapons' aim and damage, while their keen blades were ready to cleave the opponents.

History
In, one such ax belonged to Barud Barzam, a duergar leader who was in charge of an underground outpost in the River Caves, where the Black Raven River spills from under the Spine of the World. Barud offered his duergar-forged axe to anyone willing to descend into the lower caverns underneath the outpost to exterminate hook horrors and mustard jellies who infested the area.

## Durm Goroy

Durm Goroy was a convicted criminal from the city of Baldur's Gate in the late 15 century DR.

History
In the, Durm Goroy was accused of an assassination attempt on Lord Enver Gortash during The Absolute Crisis. Whether this was truth or a political setup, Durm was arrested, tried, and condemned to public execution. As the Absolutist army marched on Baldur's Gate and unbeknownst to all, Lord Gortash being one of the Absolute's leaders, Durm, alongside a fellow prisoner named Vince Torell, was to be transferred from the Stonyeyes Precinct of the Flaming Fist to the Basilisk Gate prison on the orders from Manip Farthingale. Basilisk Gate had a handy plaza with a hanging stage set up for public executions at the time.

## Dulgrath's Finest

Dulgrath's Finest was a variety of exceedingly rare wine made by Baroness Dulgrath of Aglarond in the late 14 century DR.

History
In the, Baroness Dulgrath attended the year-end celebration festival in the town of Mossbridges in the Vast. She participated in a wine-tasting and competition with her so-called "Dulgrath's Finest," aged in barrels made of enchanted Yuirwood wood. Lady Dulgrath was not a professional vintner and did not export the wine out of Aglarond, but she received many compliments on the drink she created. Dulgrath brought several casks of her wine for the competition but was extremely protective of the batch, refusing to serve Dulgrath's Finest to adventurers. It was unrecorded if Dulgrath's Finest won the competition.

Interior
The place was newly built as of, and had a lounge that was downright palatial. The lounge's ceiling was whitedaub, with a dragon motif, and had holes, most likely for peeping.

Services
According to Arauntar, the house was highcoin, that is, expensive, and was likely to sell foodstuffs. The stewards there served alcohol, amongst other amenities. Orthil thought people were almost certainly staying there, strongly implying the place was an inn.

The place was newly built as of .

Late that year, Orthil Voldovan's caravan stopped there, looking for reinforcements after suffering a series of ambushes on its way from Scornubel to Waterdeep. While he was there, Voldovan was slain and replaced by Thavaun, a Red Wizard.

At some point in the, Duver Rillyn became afflicted by lycanthropy, the affliction that led him into the service of the Kozakuran vampire Cazador Szarr, the lord of the Szarr Palace in Baldur's Gate. Along with Gheris Hhune, Blaise Hhune, and other werewolves, Duver massacred a party full of influential guests at the Szarr Palace, shortly before Cazador attempted to perform the ascension ritual. ## Durexter Dagohnlar

Durexter Dagohnlar was a merchant from Marsember in the late 14 century DR.

Description
The man was overweight, and often had bad breath because of his taste for spicy food.

Personality
He liked garlic and Thayan pepper. According to his wife, he was a brute, a boor, and thoroughly dishonest.

Possessions
He owned a large home on Calathanter Street, three blocks away from Haelithtorntowers, where many servants worked. In his bedroom, which had a tiled floor, the large bed had a high canopy of rubyweave draperies, bedposts, silken sheets, cushions; there was a glass-fronted wardrobe with frilled lovegowns and robes, a footstool, a chest-of-blankets that smelled of moth-powder. As of the early spring of, their room also sported a new Athkatlan rug, thick and fur-like. Their bedroom window opened directly over the canal. He was wealthy enough that he could afford to rip one of his wife's robes in an adventurous act of ardor.

The servants' quarters had peep-panels on the doors. The bedroom was upstairs, separated from the ground floor by at least three flights of stairs; the stair leading to the front door was in a room with a vaulted ceiling, where a crown-of-candles chandelier hung close to the top flight of stairs. Between the top flight and the middle flight of stairs was at least one copper pot with ring-handles, on which a fern grew. Between the middle flight of stairs and the last one closest to the front doors, there was an ancestral Dagohnlar set of decorative armor.

Activities
Durexter was a merchant of ill repute. He made it a point to avoid dealings with even more disreputable merchants.

Relationships
Durexter was married to Starmara Dagohnlar.

History
At some point before the spring of the, Durexter Dagohnlar had swindled two other Marsemban merchants, Aumon Tholant Bezrar and Malakar Surth, of a large sum by reneging on his obligations in an illicit deal, confident they would not have recourse from anyone else. They invaded his home one night in the early spring of that year, quickly binding and gagging him and his wife while they were otherwise occupied in their bedroom.

While they were bound, the thief Narnra Shalace burst in through their bedroom window trying to escape the Harper Glarasteer Rhauligan, in hot pursuit of her. He vociferously denied to his captors that those two were hired by him; it didn't matter, as they tried to fight Glarasteer, only to be quickly defeated. Nobody rescued him and his wife, however, leaving them to try and chew through the bindings.

Past morning he was not done chewing through the bindings. He was later visited by the local watch, but adamantly refused to reveal anything about the matter to the captain, a watchman with a grizzled moustache. He was gagged and taken from the room for that, almost certainly for questioning.

## Dungeon magazine 22

Dungeon issue 22 was released in March/April of 1990. It contained the following articles related to the Forgotten Realms:

Letters
 p.2-3
The Dungeon magazine letters column. One of the answered letters writes in about the Forgotten Realms set adventure The Ship of Night, from Dungeon #20, pointing out that the unique wand of petrification featured therein was never detailed and asks for information on it. The editors respond by providing a detailed rundown of the wand's description and powers.

The Leopard Men

 By David Howery, illustrated by Lucy Synk, Cartography by Diesel. p. 13-27
The wicked Leopard Men might have loads of treasure - but no one has ever met one and lived to talk about it. A 14-page AD&D adventure for a party of 5-7 characters totaling 54 levels. In a land of untamed jungles the PCs are hired by the wealthiest merchant of a trading outpost fort to put an end to a local cult that is preventing him from establishing a trade route with a local tribe.

Originally derived from a home-campaign set on Oerth, this setting neutral adventure can be adapted to the Forgotten Realms setting, with the adventure location replaced with Chult. Notably, this adventure frequently references the Wilderness Survival Guide and utilizes monster statistics from Dragon #122's articles "Gaming the Dark Continent" and "Out of Africa".
