## Durbem

Durbem was a dwarf artisan of the Goblinbreaker Clan who resided in Baldur's Gate in the late 14 century DR.

History
Borador Goldhand and Durbem both were clan-brothers from the Goblinbreakers, the clan that got into a large debt with some elves, though it was not known who exactly or why. During his time in Baldur's Gate in the late 14 century DR, the same time as Borador took on an adventuring career to help their clan pay off the debt, Durbem offered his artisanal weapons and armor smithing for hire. One of his business partners was merchant Bartley, who operated in the Upper City and the Lower City of the Gate.

He could often be seen slurping ale at the Purple Wyrm Inn and Tavern of the Upper City. His grumpy attitude could easily be fixed by buying him a drink and making a crude joke. He also came to possess a key to a treasure vault within the famed Halls of the Hammer, an abandoned dwarf hold in the High Moor, which he was happy to sell to a capable adventurer with whom he shared a tankard or ten. Durbem also collected an impressive amount of gold from Borador Goldhand to pay for the clan and, eventually, led his clan-brother to retake Gandam's Hold and its mine to aid further with the debt. Eventually, Durbem helped Borador Goldhand reach the status of the dwarven hero.

## Dune hag

Dune hags were monstrous humanoids related to the annis hag, who inhabited deserts and wastelands.
Description
Dune hags appeared to be humanoid female crone, tall and decrepit, looming over most any other humanoid creature. They had greenish-brown skin tone, gray hair, and brown decaying teeth. They wore ragged and soiled robes and burnoose cloaks. These creatures stood at  tall and weighed around .
Personality
Avoiding direct confrontations, dune hag lured their victims to remote locations where a covey of hags, ogres, and other evil giants could ambush and murder the prey, trick the victims into killing themselves, or just lure the prey into a sense of security and devour them in their sleep. The most common trick used by the dune hags was changing their appearance to that of a beautiful woman and luring the prey away for a tryst. Dune hag's kill eluded the victim's mind, bewitching them, and, if discovered, a disguised dune hag would try to kiss at least one victim before retreating. The kissed and enthralled victims were then lured away and consumed at a later time.
Dune hags spoke Common and Giant languages.
Abilities
Dune hags often used magical disguises, such as the disguise self spell to enthrall their victims.
Combat
When forced into a direct battle, dune hags used their clawed hands to rend the opponents and tried to grapple and drag them close for a kiss.
To no surprise, dune hags possessed an army or magical abilities. They could cast disguise self, haboob, and skin of the cactus spells thrice per day each and hallucinatory terrain once per day. All of their spells were cast at the proficiency of a seventh circle of magic.
Society
Humanoid meat was dune hags' favorite meal. They went to great lengths, ignoring great risks in pursuit of that delicacy.
History
In the, a group of Hag Survivors that included Mayrina, Adrielle, and Kled, researched ways to save children consumed by hags as the method of procreation. The diary kept by the group collected biology, anatomy, and various diagrams of many types of hags, including annis, dune, and green hags. The book also held research on the hag's bane potion that could be used to "abort" the hag pregnancy, forcing them to vomit the child they consumed.

Duna wore a simple set of chainmail armor.

History
In the, Duna Duckfens, along with other duergar, giant spiders, and drow, was slaughtered in the home of Bloomridge Park groundskeeper, Lavernica. Duna's body was left broken and decapitated on the cabin's floor. The murder spree was performed by Orin the Red and her Murder Tribunal of Bhaal.

## Durinbold & Oathoon

Durinbold & Oathoon was a shop that supplied provisions to businesses of Baldur's Gate in the late 15 century DR.

History
In the, during the Absolute crisis, Durinbold & Oathoon faced supply chain difficulties due to caravans not being able to freely enter the city. Martnev Oathoon, Valued Customer Disciplinarian, sent out a notification letter to Oliver Tefoco of The Bee Hive in the Lower City, informing him of the suspension of food and dry goods deliveries that would eventually renew when the crisis ended. During the same year, Pammella Nortale of Nortale's Hostel, was in debt to Durinbold & Oathoon and missed a month's payment. The provisioners demanded her to make a minimum payment or be tuned over to the Fist Repossessions.

## Durlim

Durlim was one of the bandits under the Iron Fox in the mid–8 century DR.

Possessions
The men of the Iron Fox wore light armor and brought no lances or horses.

History
When Elminster Aumar, a Chosen of Mystra, carried out a scouring of the bandits in Buckralam's Starn, Durlim remained with his boss, the Iron Fox, until moments before his death at Fox Tower. They were ambushed at the mess hall, where Elminster almost struck him down with a crossbow quarrel to the face moments after he was ordered to the gallery; as a veteran, Durlim managed to block the quarrel with his gauntlet. He was then sent down the gallery. The Iron Fox died while trying to regroup with the man, without learning what became of him.

## Dun'Robar Weapon Master set

The Dun'Robar Weapon Master set were a varied collection of enchanted masterwork weapons produced and used by House Dun'Robar of Sschindylryn.

Description
The weapons were universally exceptional-quality and matte black implements. It included drow-style weapons like bastard swords, daggers, greataxes, khopeshes, light maces, longswords, mauls, quarterstaves, rapiers, scimitars, shortswords, and warhammers, as well as dwarven waraxes made for duergar soldiers. Though their craftsmanship was some of the best in Faerûn, few would be foolish enough to try to steal one from those who possessed them.

Powers
The Dun'Robar weapons were made even more deadly with a variety of magical enhancements that improved the strikes and tactics that the wielder could use.

History
Many of these weapons were destroyed or stolen by Cormyrean forces when they raided House Dun'Robar as a counterattack during the Sschindylryn invasion of Eveningstar. The loss of these weapons and their house members severely damaged Dun'Robar's reputation in the city, weakening the alliance between them and House Szind and House Avithoul that led the drow war effort.

## Dungeons & Dragons Online/Disciples of Shadow

Disciples of Shadow is a partially free quest chain for Dungeons & Dragons Online where the player characters must investigate Sharran cultists in Cormyr. The adventure pack was added to the game on May 22, 2013 as part of Update 18, and serves as an introduction to further content involving Wheloon Prison and the Shadovar.

The final part of Disciples of Shadow requires the purchase of Dungeons & Dragons Online: Shadowfell Conspiracy.

Plot

Disciples of Shar has Abella Toth pursuing the Sharran Dedryk Black, who somehow escaped from Wheloon Prison. She's traced him to a secret hideout in the Storm Horns, where he's already assembled a cult of fanatical followers. Abella tasks the players with finding him and bringing him back. After fighting through the cave system, they manage to corner and defeat Dedryk, who allows himself to be captured.

Escape Plan features Barthold Moncrief, who hires the players to help guard the Wheloon Prison boat as it transports Dedryk to Wheloon Prison. While camped on the shore of the Wyvernflow one night, the escort comes under attack by a fierce force of lizardfolk led by Rynarr Nosryrr and Srass Marnanr, and they dimension door Dedryk away. Before they can undo his bindings properly and make a full escape, the players pursue them to a nearby campsite and recapture Dedryk, once again putting him in custody.

Shadow of a Doubt is from Celia Pendrick, who has the players take part in Dedryk's interrogation once they reach Wheloon Prison. Joining Cornelius Latrimor in the Magistrate's Office, they manage to get some information out of him but are interrupted by the Shadowfell Nexus bringing the Shadowfell to the Prime Material plane, and allowing a horde of shadows to emerge. While attempting to get help from Captain Hillard, the players find that the shadows are accompanied by a full-blown riot and prisoner breakout attempt. Though they manage to secure the area and end the crisis, the shadar-kai Teralyn takes advantage of the distraction to kill Magistrate Cornelius and vanish Dedryk away into the shadows.

## Dungeons & Dragons Online/Web of Chaos

Web of Chaos is a free quest chain for Dungeons & Dragons Online where the player characters face strange drow cultists in Stormreach, only to discover Lolth and her plots for both Eberron and Faerûn. The adventure pack was added to the game on February 27, 2012 as part of Update 16, and served as an introduction to Menace of the Underdark and further Forgotten Realms content. It was also accompanied by the novel Skein of Shadows, which involves the cult of the Spinner of Shadows and Tarath Marad.

Plot

The Lords of Dust has the players be recruited by Brother Dectaran to help raid the cultist base in Stormreach, having identified it as a Lords of Dust hideout. They're joined by Inquisitor Gnomon and Novice Corin Dryden, and proceed to cut a swathe through the cultists inside. In a shocking twist, Gnomon turns out to be a rakshasa in disguise, having infiltrated the Church of the Silver Flame and sabotaged their efforts until then while working on a fiendish conspiracy. Though Gnomon and Karas are defeated, their ritual succeeds in opening the Road to Tarath Marad into Khyber, allowing their plan to proceed.

Servants of the Overlord has Felnis send the players into the blasted-open pit to fight through the cultists as they try to break into the Spinner's Prison. Along the way the players fight many drow cultists, as well as fiendish spiders, jariliths, hezrou, fire reavers, and hell hounds. They eventually find Brother Aurelius, the Silver Flame faithful assigned to protecting the prison.

In The Spinner of Shadows Orrex sends the players into the Spinner's Prison where they encounter the Spinner of Shadows herself. With the Silver Flame Guardian weakened by the flame eater spiders, the Spinner is freed from her bonds and immediately turns on the Lords of Dust that saved her. Before she can escape, the players and the Silver Flame Guardian manage to relight the wards and trap her once more, only for Lolth to personally intervene and open the Rift Between Worlds. The Spinner escapes with stolen dragonshards, leaving Eberron behind.

Beyond the Rift has the players return to the Spinner's Prison, where the Rift Between Worlds continues to grow and will eventually pull Eberron into the Demonweb Pits. Tavnar Kronnak of the Gatekeepers sends the players into the Demonweb, hoping to find a solution to the growing rift. Once in the Abyss the players are fortunate enough to meet Elminster, who was also investigating the strange goings-on with Lolth. He saves them from a bebilith and guides them for a time before departing, and the players fight their way to a portal exit that leads to Faerûn's Underdark. Encountering the drow of Sschindylryn amid their invasion of Eveningstar, the players eventually reach the surface in Cormyr and rescue a number of Evenor villagers from the Eveningstar Cavern, including Ana Brabener.

## Dungeons & Dragons Online/Search and Rescue

Search and Rescue is a free-to-play stand-alone quest for Dungeons & Dragons Online where the player characters look for lost adventurers in the Stonelands. The adventure was added to the game on June 1, 2016 as part of Update 31.

Plot
The players find Popkin Shortshanks in Eveningstar, a gnome in desperate need of assistance. She was adventuring with her friends in the Stonelands, but they were overcome by foes and need immediate rescue. Thanks to some word of recall-infused trinkets given by a "nice old gent with a weird hat", it's possible to rescue her friends as soon as they are found. In the Stonelands, the entry to the tomb is surrounded by typical inhabitants of the wilderness like scorpions and harpies, and the interior filled by the ancient undead. The mummy Laeron is roused and does battle, and a secret tunnel behind his throne shows the way to deeper caverns and the Underdark where Popkin's friends were trapped.

Fortunately, Jarus, Helayna, and Kirina are merely isolated and hiding, though Liantor Stoneborn was slain and looted by Clan Daeronnax kobolds. At the end of the tunnels is the lair of the red dragon Daeronnax, who has kept Tristran of Daggerford as a snack for later, and who can be rescued. Thus, all but one of Popkin's friends can be saved.

Down an optional branching route is a secret Drow outpost of Sschindylryn ruled by Mistress Laerion'vyr, who has a particular interest in summoning slaadi for her forces, including Il'tharis the Terrible.

Background
A dead phaerimm can be found crushed beneath rocks in this adventure. The phaerimm model would have been originally produced for the earlier cancelled adventure pack Heart of Netheril, which would have concluded the Shadowfell Conspiracy plotline.

Shadow Under Thunderholme is an adventure pack for Dungeons & Dragons Online where the player characters delve into the long-lost dwarven ruins of Thunderholme and face the Cult of the Dragon and the shadow dragon Aurgloroasa. The adventure pack was added to the game on March 10, 2014 as part of Update 21.

## Dungeons & Dragons Online/The High Road of Shadows

The High Road of Shadows is an adventure pack for Dungeons & Dragons Online where the player characters walk the High Road of Cormyr, hunting for the pieces of a Nether Scroll. The adventure pack was added to the game on November 12th, 2012 as part of Update 16, and connects the two main stories of Shadowfell Conspiracy.

Content
Netherese Legacy

The Netherese Legacy quest chain takes place after the Shadow Over Wheloon quest chain of Shadowfell Conspiracy, in the wake of Oriphaun Huntsilver's escape from Wheloon Prison with the Nether Scroll. A team of Harpers arrive in Eveningstar to follow his trail and find the pieces of the Nether Scroll that he has hidden along the High Road west of town, and the Shadovar are also hot on the trail of their artifact. While the Purple Dragons and War Wizards are busy with the Sschindylryn invasion of Eveningstar, opportunistic bandits led by Theodas Riveron and werewolves from the King's Forest take advantage of the situation to run rampant. The leader of the Harpers, Rina Belostos, is responsible for giving the quest chain. In addition to the quests, the High Road is littered with Oriphaun's journal entries, describing his thoughts and experiences as he evades the Shadovar and hides the Scroll pieces.

Detour from Tibalt Dormen sends the players to meet Bensen Virandson at Greenweald Lane. They must escort him and his piece of the Nether Scroll past a small army of hired mercenaries (including the double-agent Nil) and reach the Harper Lodge for safe storage. The leader of the mercenaries, Dom, beats the Harpers there but is overcome.

In Rest Stop, Freda Gambosi sends the players to Beckside Castle, where Oriphaun has hidden another piece. The ruins are inhabited by a number of adventurers using it as a camping spot, unaware of the Netherese agents elsewhere in the structure. After finding the Netherese forces, the players overcome the arcanists Keldan Shadeskein and Gaston Wyrst, taking the Scroll for the Harpers.

Lost in the Swamp takes the players from Eradin Krall to the wisp-haunted Lost Souls' Swamp where the hermit Seamus lives, only to find the medusa Sshaastess has already taken over the swamp and seized the Nether Scroll piece, dominating elementals and corrupting the local dryads. After fighting through the creatures defending Sshaastess, the medusa is slain and the Scroll recovered. The players may also help the titular lost souls of the swamp—Callie Brennan, Evrim Whitehair, and Kyle Briarbuck, who were trapped by a powerful shadow.

In A Stay at the Inn, Kordo Sweetspire sends players to the Old Man's Face inn of Tyrluk. Oriphaun hid a piece there and although the Netherese have already taken over the inn, they are unable to find it. After eliminating the Netherese commander Tarys, the bartender William Windsphere reveals that the only thing Oriphaun left was a bar tab—actually the Nether Scroll, flipped over and scribbled on. Thus the players are able to recover that piece of the artifact.

The End of the Road has Olyn Araphyr dispatch players to meet Girmond Dyle, holder of the Harper's pieces, before heading to Granwell Keep in the Blooded Wood, where the Netherese have gathered up all the remaining pieces of the Nether Scroll. On arrival at the Keep, the players find Girmond dead and the Netherese in possession of all of the pieces. Before the players confront the arcanists, they accidentally cause the Scroll to open a portal to the Abyss, causing the glabrezu Razagnol and his dretches to invade. After a fierce battle, Rina Belostos and Elminster arrive to take custody of the Scroll and ensure that it doesn't fall into the wrong hands.

## Dungeons & Dragons Online/The Haunted Halls of Eveningstar

The Haunted Halls of Eveningstar is an adventure pack for Dungeons & Dragons Online where the player characters revisit the classic Haunted Halls adventure. The adventure pack was added to the game on March 10, 2014 as part of Update 21.

The adventure is narrated by Ed Greenwood, the original creator of the Haunted Halls, and features optional voiceover commentary from him about the dungeon.

## Dusk's Dark Desires

A Tour of Tempest was an Umberlant book that discussed the divine tempest domain, written by and published sometime in or before the late 15 century DR.

Content
Dusk's Dark Desires was a series of intrigue and lewd romance chapbooks. One of the later novels in the series was noticeably more smutty than its prequels with the blood and sex being, arguably the books' best parts. Some also praised the book's fine intrigue and character work of Bedford, a beautifully-written werecow, as well as Dusk, the novels' protagonist.

History
In the, the Book Brigade, a book club in the city of Baldur's Gate, met to discuss several books in the series.

## Dungeons & Dragons Miniatures

Dungeons & Dragons Miniatures was a line of pre-painted plastic miniatures that were produced by Wizards of the Coast between 2003 and 2011, with sets releasing roughly every four months. They were produced for the 3.5 and 4 editions of Dungeons & Dragons, as well as Dungeons & Dragons Miniatures Game, commonly referred to as DDM, and later found reuse in multiple boardgames. Elements of the Forgotten Realms setting featured in a number of the line's sets.

Description
D&D Miniatures departed from previous miniature lines in that their miniatures were pre-painted, made of plastic, and sold in randomized blind box collectible packs. Miniatures went through several lines of concept art before manufacturing. Each miniature came with a two-sided stat card detailing statistics, with the stats on one side being for the skirmish game and the other for the 3.5 edition of the tabletop game and later, beginning with Dungeons of Dread, statistics for 4 edition. Early on the side featuring the tabletop statistics also included the concept art sketch for the miniature. With the cancellation of the D&D Miniatures Game in late 2008, sets that came after Demonweb featured only statistics for the tabletop game on their stat-cards.

The miniatures of each set were divided into three rarities: common, uncommon, and rare. Each rarity was denoted by a marking on the bottom of a miniature's base. Those of "rare" rarity were given far more attention to detail in their painting than other of lower rarities. In the last major set released for the line, Lords of Madness, a fourth rarity was introduced – very rare.

For a short time between 2009 and 2010 sets were marketed under the names "Monster Manual" and "Player's Handbook Heroes". The former were partially blind randomized packs, with a single figure visible, while the latter were non-random and exclusively featured miniatures of the various playable races and character classes. For the last year of the line's lifespan, miniatures would revert to being sold in completely random blind boxes.

A roleplaying supplement was released in the line's debut year, the Miniatures Handbook, for use with 3.5 edition. This supplement offered further advice on integrating miniatures into the tabletop game, as well as mass combat rules.

History
In June of 2002, Wizards of the Coast announced on their website that they would be ceasing production of their Chainmail Miniatures Game come August and hinted at having a new miniatures game in the works that would utilize the Dungeons & Dragons brand. The replacement to Chainmail would eventually be revealed the next year as the D&D Miniatures line and game, as well as a supplement titled Miniatures Handbook. The D&D Miniatures game would be a simplified & streamlined version of Chainmail, being primarily the work of designers Jonathan Tweet, Rob Heinsoo, and Skaff Elias.

The miniature line's first announced entry would be the eighty-miniature set Harbinger, to be released on September 26, followed shortly thereafter by the Miniatures Handbook in October and the expansion Dragoneye in November to coincide with the release of Draconomicon: The Book of Dragons. Even in this early stage, Wizards announced their intentions to produce creatures from the Forgotten Realms campaign setting, as well as other settings like Eberron and Dragonlance. And that the RPGA Network was going to focus on utilizing D&D Miniatures within their upcoming roleplay events. In September, prior to the release of Harbinger, Dragon #311 came out with a Sneak Preview article talking about the new miniatures list and came with a sample challenge for the skirmish game.

In an interview with the Miniature Handbook's designers, posted on the Wizards of the Coast website on September 13, Jonathan Tweet stated that the impetus behind the development of both the supplement and the D&D Miniatures line in general was repeated observations that miniatures seemed to be integral to the Dungeons & Dragons experience and they wanted to support players who utilized them.

In 2006, the D&D Miniatures line began to strain under increasing costs of manufacturing, eventually leading Wizards of the Coast to make the decision to increase the price of packs.

In early 2008 it was announced that all subsequent releases of D&D Miniatures would be designed for use with 4 edition, beginning with the set Dungeons of Dread in April, and that all existing miniatures would receive new stat-cards making them compatible with the new edition. This would be preceded by the set Desert of Desolation in January, which notably featured every monster that was to appear in the upcoming Monster Manual. The D&D Miniatures Game in turn would receive revisions to maintain continuity with the new rules.

Later in October of that same year, Wizards of the Coast announced their decision to change how D&D Miniatures were packaged, now intending to sell sets in packs subtitled Monster Manual and Player's Handbook Heroes, but also to decrease the amount of figures per booster from eight to five. Two days later, it was announced that they would be ceasing support for the D&D Miniatures Game. A disclosed reason for this was that sanctioned play of the game was seeing decreased attendance. A further undisclosed reason for this cancellation was that Wizards was then experimenting with new miniatures-focused games. These would come to be released as the D&D Adventure System, a line of cooperative dungeon crawl boardgames. These began with Castle Ravenloft in 2010, which came with D&D Miniatures.

At D&D Experience 2010, Wizards of the Coast announced that they would be ceasing the division of sets into Monster Manual and Player's Handbook Heroes packs, as well as their decision to revert back to their prior method of packaging miniatures in entirely randomized packs.

On January 12, 2011, Wizards of the Coast announced on their official website the immediate end of the D&D Miniatures line, ending with the set Lords of Madness. They clarified their intentions to continue releasing special collector's sets for a time and to repurpose their stock for other products, such as subsequent releases in the D&D Adventure System line, Wrath of Ashardalon and The Legend of Drizzt.

Player's Handbook Heroes
This series consisted of twelve booster packs of non-random miniatures, all sculpted by Peter Lee, three a piece in transparent packaging along with an accompanying power card. The miniatures represented various character races and classes that were available in the 4 edition of Dungeons & Dragons, derived from options available in the Player's Handbook and Player's Handbook 2, as well as some options from the Forgotten Realms Player's Guide and the Eberron Player's Guide.

## Duethaea Lauraunfeir

Duethaea Lauraunfeir  was a Chosen of Mystra of the Delimbiyr Vale in the 9 century DR.

Description
She was tall and very skinny, more hard-bitten than beautiful, and often dressed in a rigid leather greatcoat that reached her ankles.

Personality
She was spirited and difficult, but gregarious enough to mingle. She also liked very strong drink.

Abilities
Duethaea was strong in the Art.

Possessions
She wore two enchanted bracers that launched a number of spells.

Relationships
Her family saw her as difficult. She had been trained by Elminster Aumar.

Activities
She liked to mingle in taverns all around the Savage North.

History
Duethaea met her match in the Battle of Blades Afire, where she attacked heedlessly and was ultimately mortally wounded by the demonic foes. She survived until the end, but later died of her injuries in Elminster's arms, spilling silver fire on the battlefield.

## Duddy Dudderken

Duddy Dudderken was an inhabitant of the Lower City of Baldur's Gate in the late 15 century DR.

Description
Duddy was a human boy with a light olive skin tone, short, curly dark brown hair, and dark brown eyes.

History
In the, as the Cult of the Absolute menaced the city of Baldur's Gate, Duddy Dudderken attended a performance of some showy magics by three spellslingers, on the Murl's Rest plaza in front of Sorcerous Sundries. The boy obnoxiously yelled demands of bigger and louder magics.

## Dunbradar cheese

Dunbradar cheese was a variety of goat's cheese produced in the town of Dunbridges in the Border Kingdoms of south Faerûn.

Description
Dunbradar cheese was made with goat milk and had a distinct sharp flavor. Another notable feature was the use of various nuts, mixed in with the cheese.

## Dungeons & Dragons: Builders of Baldur's Gate

Dungeons & Dragons: Builders of Baldur's Gate is a board game set in the Forgotten Realms setting and the city of Baldur's Gate. The game is centered around faction competition between the Council of Four, the Flaming Fist, and The Guild for building and controlling the city. The game is designed for two to five players.

## Duchess Wyndrake

The Duchess of Wyndrake was a noblewoman living near Neverwinter in the late years of the 15 century DR.Labelled only as "the Duchess of Wyndrake", she is technically unnamed.

History
Around 1491 DR, Duchess Wyndrake, a wealthy patron of Neverwinter, requested aid at her manor. She told the Hero of Sleeping Dragon Bridge of an unnatural fog that had been closing in around her lands, and of the arrival of travelers called the Vistani that many believed to be responsible.

## Dunscale

Dunscales, or dunscale lizards, were a type of reptile native to the Shaar.

Behavior
Dunscales spent most of their time sunbathing lazily. However, they were capable of sudden great speed, making them fast enough to catch their prey. They were even quicker to devour any food that did not require them to chase it.

Ecology
Dunscales hunted insects, rats, and small lizards. They were omnivores, and were also known to eat trash and even dung.

History
When the settlement of Rethmar first grew to a thousand people, rats arrived in great number with the caravans that passed through the city on the Trader's Way. This rat population was brought under control largely thanks to predation by the local dunscales, who had driven the rodents nearly extinct by the mid-to-late 14 century DR. The inhabitants of the city began to feed the lizards their waste and spoiled food, leading them to use the expression "tossed to the dunscales" meaning to throw something away.

## Duskmere (Count)

The Count of Duskmere was an elven nobleman of the Time of Dragons, living in the 25 millennium before Dalereckoning.

Activities
Among his activities as a minor ruler, he engaged in much treason.

Relationships
He was the father of Winterflower Duskmere, and the head of House Duskmere.

History
In the year, the elven house of Duskmere was involved in a treasonous attempt to strike at the forces of King Orchtrien of Dawnfire. These rebel forces almost won, but were personally routed by Prince Bexendral, who teleported to their aid. To punish the elves for their treachery, King Orchtrien had them send their sons to his court, where they would be educated and serve as hostages to ensure no more treachery was attempted. One of the hostages he sent was Lady Winterflower Duskmere, his own daughter.

Later, his daughter returned to their forest, carrying a trove of the King's spellbooks. The King hid her and his court at a secret stronghold in response, biding some time to read the spellbooks. However, the stronghold was found by Orchtrien's forces, which came in Winterflower's pursuit. The stronghold was razed; the Count was almost certainly slain in the battle.

Appendix
Appearances

References

---

## Duskmere

House Duskmere was an elven noble house during the Time of Dragons.

Organization
The House was led by the Count of Duskmere.

Base of Operations
House Duskmere's territories lay near the center of the domains of King Orchtrien of Dawnfire.

Relationships
Although House Duskmere was supposedly loyal to King Orchtrien, they still plotted treason against him together with the Starfall and Vilirith houses.

History
House Duskmere was involved in a treasonous attempt to draw out soldiers of Orchtrien and slay them in . Claiming they needed aid to repel troll attacks, they drew a force led by one of the King's deputies, the elven wizard Rhespen Ash. Rhespen's forces were nearly routed, but they, and he personally, were saved by the arrival of Prince Bexendral, who teleported in and broke the rebel forces. In response to this treachery, King Orchtrien had the heirs of the three houses involved in the plot, the Starfall, Vilirith, and Duskmere, seized and brought to his home in Dawnfire for education and to serve as his hostages. House Duskmere sent as their hostage Winterflower Duskmere, the Count's daughter.

By, Winterflower Duskmere returned to them with a trove of copied spellbooks pilfered from Orchtrien. In response, the Count and his court took the house into hiding at a secret stronghold. This stronghold was eventually found and destroyed by King Orchtrien and his forces, leaving Winterflower Duskmere as the only survivor.

Appendix
Appearances

References

---

## Durnhal

Durnhal was King of Corwell in the Moonshae Isles during the mid-to-late 3 century DR.

Relationships
He and his queen, Morgan, were the first human monarchs of Corwell to use the Great Bear as their standard, making them the forebears of the Kendrick family.

History
Durnhal came to rule Corwell following the end of the long rule of the Hugh Dynasty's last regent, Cedric Riker, in the . At this time, many of the lords of the Ffolk squabbled amongst each other rather than follow the High King, and King Durnhal was among those lords who were less cooperative, inviting antagonism from High King Gwylloch.

In the, King Durnhal and Queen Morgan were captured by Gwylloch's son, Price Ketheryll, and his Doomed Legion. They were accused of high treason, and then executed alongside other captives at the Castle of Skulls. Ketheryll kept their gold signet rings as trophies.

Over a millennium later, tales and legends still recalled the royal couple, and speculated that their deaths had been the catalyst for the curse that ultimately befell Prince Ketheryll. In the, the halfling adventurer Pawldo and his companion Stefanik entered the Castle of Skulls seeking treasure. They escaped with the lost royal signet rings, and planned to gift them to High King Tristan Kendrick and his queen, Robyn Kendrick.

Appendix
References

---

## Dumathoin's bracers

Dumathoin's bracers were a pair of magical bracers from the dwarven city of Thunderholme.

Powers
The bracers offered great protection to the wearer from physical and magical threats, including freezing, burning, acidic, sonic, and shocking assaults. They also improved the wearer's agility and speed quite a bit.

History
The bracers were once used by Dumathoin Thunderstone, Captain of the Guard during an attack upon Thunderholme. The bracers were later found in the lair treasury of the shadow dragon Aurgloroasa in the 1480s DR.

## Dungeon Academy

The Dungeon Academy was a secret school for monsters located in the Star Mounts.

Location
The Dungeon Academy was located in the Star Mounts. Specifically, Akhellon Ridge in the Veiled Mountains, which were in the Endless Woods. It was  from Horntree Village.

Structure
The Dungeon Academy was an old building. Some parts of it were in poor condition. The first floor of the Academy was designed around a central corridor. Beneath that floor were the 'dungeon levels' for classes and other activities. The floors were connected by a large spiral staircase. Below, the Academy was connected to volcanic pools of magma. It had over eight subterranean floors in total, descending to caverns that were off-limits to students. The lowest floors held the secret

Interior
The Academy was filled with bats and old traps. Some parts smelled bad. while others were freezing cold. A large pendulum in the corridor was used to help mark the time. Some corridors were lined with chests, for students to use as storage.
Some notable areas included:
 Goreball Pitch: A grassy sporting field that lay outside. Students played the game goreball here.
 Dining Chamber: A room in which students ate meals like lunch. The students divided themselves up according to racial social groups.
 The Hall of Eternal Suffering and Monotony: A subterranean detention hall that was connected to the rest of the academy by a narrow bridge over a pool of magma. It served as the prison for Carrion.
 The Lurkery: A forest for students to practice their lurking in.
 The Library: An extensive six-story collection of all sorts of books.
 The Dean's Office: A well-decorated room home to the Dean.
 Noggin and Needler's Hovel: The cluttered workspace of Noggin and Needler.
 Dormitory: A series of rooms in which some students lived. They varied in size to accomodate their inhabitants; the smaller ones were positioned on top of the larger ones.
 Staff housing: A series of cottages where staff lived.
 The refuse trench: A part of the administrative wing, where the Academy's waste was incinerated by fire elementals.
 The imp shafts: A network of shafts used by the imps that mantained the Academy.
Activities
The Academy trained young monsters in the ways of being monsters before they graduated and were ultimately sent out to dungeons. Students were organized into grades, of which there were at least six. Classes taught in the academy included: Advanced Sneakcraft and Skulkery, Bartering, Common Creatures and Crawlers, Ghostly Groaning and Other Unsettling Noises, History of Horrible Humans, How to Befoul and Offend, Introduction to Hoarding, Intro to Skulking, Taking Care of Your Hoard and something called PE.

Classes were held in semesters. Some classes were known to issue homework, in the form of essays. Students were also sometimes sent out into the outside world to practice what they had learnt. Some students organized in clubs, such as the Lurking Club. Students whose parents were employed by the Academy stayed on school premises. Students also took the summer off to return to their families. The Academy celebrated Baneday and held a Mid-Never Winter break.

The Academy had a particularly strong school spirit, which manifested in support for their goreball team, the Dungeon Academy Flumphs. The Flumphs, named for the school mascot of the flumph, had an ongoing rivalry with the superior Waterdeep Dragons team from the Waterdeep School for Dragons. The Flumphs were extremely inferior to their rivals. Bullying was rampant at the school.. This included scratching offending messages on other students' property, such as "BEWARE THE FART KING".

The rules of the school were outlined in The Dungeon Academy student handbook. The Academy had banned all forms of undead and necromancy along with it. The demilich Professor Gast was the only undead allowed on school premises. Even spells like withering touch were not allowed at school.  Kindness was also banned, and when exhibited, punishable by detention. Different professors took turns monitoring students in detention. The Academy had a school council that held votes to decide on school policies.

The Academy had an exchange program with slaadi from the plane of Limbo. It participated the Tourney of Terror, a goreball tournament held every 50 years. During the Tourney, classes were suspended.

There was also a yearbook that assigned students positions such as 'least likely to change'.

Defenses
The academy was secret and well defended. The Endless Woods, which surrounded the mountains in which the academy was located, only allowed passage to those that it deemed worthy, such as students of the Academy.
It was also protected by the Nexus Marker, which existed below the school in the Akhellon Ridge. The Nexus Marker interacted with magical energies of three intersecting leylines and a rich deposit of diamonds and surrounded the school with a huge protective field that prevented teleportation and other magic.

History
The mountainous location of the academy had previously been the lair of the blue dragon Akhellon, from whom it got the name Akhellon Ridge. Some time after she left, a clan of kobolds laired there, and then a necromantic order of wizards. Adventurers explored the natural caves, seeking the Nexus Marker, and the Academy was eventually built there.

The Academy was originally a school that included dragons among its other monstrous students. Impro Vice, Zxaticus, Noggin and Needler and Cedaver had been students there. They eventually inherited the school operations and became teachers. But Zxaticus and Cedaver had a series of intense disagreements, some related to Cedaver's advisor Elgred Morbide. A schism occurred, and Cedaver left to found the Waterdeep School for Dragons.

The Academy had existed for over 300 years. Its school mascot had previously been a bulette, until 200 years ago it was changed to the flumph after a bulette attack.

The human barbarian Mad Smithy had in the past tried to take the Academy. He died in the process, but his greataxe remained as the pendulum for the clock in the corridor on the first floor.

One winter, the necromancer Lord Carrion used magic to take control of some students who had been sent on an excursion to Horntree Village. He led some of the students, along with some villagers, through a portal. He was stopped by the newly formed Danger Club and the adventurer Allidora Steelstrike. Carrion was kept as a prisoner at the academy

Months later,the Tourney of Terror was held in the Academy. Carrion escaped, and threatened the school. In response, Dean Zxaticus moved the student population to outdoor tents and continued with the tournament. But the tournament was attacked by skeletons and undead treants. Carrion escaped with his ally Elgred Morbide through a portal. The tournament was suspended, the Waterdeep Dragons were declared winners, and the students spent the night outdoors while the staff searched the school for the necromancers. In the abandoned chambers of the old school, The Danger Club faced off against Carrion and Elgred Morbide, who revealed themselves to be servants of the dracolich An'Kizhek. Morbide escaped through a portal, escaping with the keystone of the Nexus Marker, abandoning Carrion. Without the barrier created by the Marker, the school was undefended. Zxaticus and Cedaver put some of their differences aside to protect their students.

Rumors & Legends
Among the students of the Dungeon Academy, it was rumored that an ochre jelly lived in the highest room of the Academy.

Inhabitants
The Academy taught students of a variety of monstrous races including slaadi, gnolls, dao, myconids, mimics, owlbears, oozes, kuo-toa, goblins, bugbears, flail snails, and dretches. The staff of the Academy were equally varied and strange.
It should be noted that some of these creatures, like owlbears and oozes, are normally non-intelligent and therefore incapable of learning.

Notable Students
 Zellidora Stormclash, a human who disguised herself as a minotaur.
 Hugo, an owlbear.
 Bauble, a mimic.
 Snabla, a kobold.
 Gixi, a slaad.
 Jizek, a dao.
 Gutrash, a goblin.
 Patty, a bugbear.
 Bloppy, an ooze.

Notable Staff
 Dean Zxaticus, a beholder.
 Professor Gast, a hobgoblin demilich who taught History of Horrible Humans.
 Professor Kifin Stormclash, a minotaur who taught Goreball.
 Professor Cantrip, a gelatinous cube that had merged with the intelligence of an alchemist it had half-digested.
 Professor Impro Vice, a mimic professor of alchemy.
 Professor Viletongue, a night hag.
 Professor Screamshatter, who taught Intro to Skulking.
 Nihildris, a mind flayer counsellor.
 Shinka Bookbinder, a winged kobold librarian.
 Mavis, a fire elemental cook.
 Durg, Gageth, and Ergakk, cyclops groundskeepers.
 Noggin and Needler, an ettin healer.

The minotaur Iasme Stormclash, while not an employee of the school, was Kifin's partner. She also resided on school grounds. The blink dog Flash was also brought to the Academy by the Danger Club.

The deepest caverns of the academy were home to violent skeletons.

## Dungeons & Dragons: The Queen's Cradle

"Dungeons & Dragons: The Queen's Cradle" is an episode of Secret Level, an animated anthology series with each episode based on the setting of a different video game or role-playing game. It was released on December 10, 2024, on the Amazon Prime Video streaming service and is the first episode of the first season. Only 16 minutes long, it revolves around a band of adventurers clashing with the Cult of the Dragon.

Synopsis
A group of adventurers—Mora, a goliath paladin of Torm; Tally, a gnome wizard; Luzum, a gold dwarf monk; and Ahokal, a half-orc druid—attack a Cult of the Dragon cell one evening and rescue a boy named Solon, who is apparently being taken to be sacrificed at the Well of Dragons. His body is tattooed with Iokharic script and he hears voices speaking in Draconic. Mora speaks of her violent past and reformed nature to Solon. She gifts him her gold symbol of Torm.

Seeking aid, they go to the gold dragon Oriel. Outside his lair, Dragon Cultists led by a lich attack and are held off by the heroes. Inside, Oriel removes the evil force inside Solon and absorbs it. But Oriel is consumed by it from within, transforming into an avatar of the dragon goddess Tiamat. She dragon breath attacks the group, but Solon displays cleric powers with the holy symbol of Torm and wards off her flame. The adventurers stay to confront Tiamat. There the story ends.

## Dungeons & Dragons Online/Fall of the Night Brigade

Fall of the Night Brigade is an adventure pack for Dungeons & Dragons Online where the player characters return to Wheloon Prison to help the Riverside Rats gang against the murderous Night Brigade. The adventure pack was added to the game on December 11, 2024 as part of Update 71.

Plot

The Fall of the Night Brigade quest chain is introduced by Old Tarlow, a representative of the Riverside Rats. They've been driven out of their hideout and lost many friends, and want nothing more than to break back into the prison and return home. To do so, the Sharran Night Brigade gang must first be destroyed.

Rats in a Cage starts off with Ryndalla Cuttail sending the players to rescue captured wererats and deal with Agarta the Breaker in her arena complex. Using a ratty talisman, they are able to take a smaller form and sneak past the Night Brigade defenses and strike from the inside, beating up the gladiators Gregori the Broken, Bonegnasher the Cursed, Durvastra, and finally the bloodthirsty Agarta, putting an end to her murderous games.

The Wizard's Labyrinth has Wyshbryn Saldar target the insane wizard Darstil Vurusk for death. His tower has become a labyrinth of death and torture, with his test subjects doomed without aid. Once again sneaking in as rats, the players are dropped into his laboratory maze, which they eventually escape. The mad scientist is eventually cornered and slain, ending his reign of terror.

The Knife's Edge is led by Neese the Burrower, who wishes to see Master Thief Karissa the Knife fall. The greedy thief has laired deep underneath the city in a palatial hideout with all her stolen riches. Despite the many traps, puzzles, and guards defending her, Karissa is unable to escape her fate at the paws of vengeful rats.

The Darkness and the Light ends the quests with Garmor the Grim, who sends the players to take care of the Night Brigade leader, the priestess Storna Harnwood. She is fortified in the locked-down Temple of Shar, which used to be the Riverside Rats' own hideout. Meeting the auspicious Selûnite Brunwell Godber outside, the players' moon-blessed mission sees them dispel Shar's darkness and overcome Storna's defenses. With Storna and the other officers gone, the Night Brigade are no more and the Riverside Rats can return safely home.

## Durvastra

Durvastra was a cloud giant who met an untimely end in the depths of Wheloon Prison in the late 15 century DR.

History
During the operation of the Wheloon Prison, the ruthless Night Brigade enjoyed a period of ascendancy above the other gangs. One of their officers, Agarta the Breaker, operated a series of arenas where gladiatorial beasts and prisoners were pitted against each other. The most extraordinary of her fighters was the cloud giant Durvastra, who somehow ended up in the prison, where he served as a champion gladiator for some time. This grim winning streak was ended when the Riverside Rats gang sent a team into the prison to fight the Night Brigade, eventually meeting Durvastra in his arena and slaying him and Agarta as well.The implication may be that Durvastra was one of the giants that served Archwizard Amskar in Shadowfell Conspiracy, making him part of the forces of Thultanthar and thus worthy of being placed in Wheloon Prison.

## Dungeons & Dragons: The Tyrant's Eye

Dungeons & Dragons: The Tyrant’s Eye is a pinball machine, based on the Forgotten Realms setting, that was designed & manufactured by Stern Pinball, Inc. to coincide with the 50 anniversary of Dungeons & Dragons. It was released in 2025.

Description
The Tyrant's Eye cabinet was produced in three variations: Pro, Premium, and Limited Edition. Each is visually distinguished by different decals that were hand-drawn by illustrator Vincent Proce. The Pro cabinet depicts the game's adventuring company facing off against Xanathar and his pet Sylgar, the Premium cabinet depicts the dragonborn Ardor riding a gold dragon against the red dragon Rath the Relentless, and the Limited Edition depicts Tiamat and Xanathar. The latter is also distinguished by having a reflective foil treatment on its decal.

All three cabinets depict the same playing field, an abstract map of Mid-West Faerûn, featuring a number of previously established locations like Arabel. It also features hazards in the form of a gelatinous cube, which traps balls through use of an electromagnet, and the dragon Rath the Relentless. Rath's head is a mechanical fixture with illuminated eyes. In the Premium & Limited Edition cabinets, Rath's head features multi-axis motion, and omnidirectional impact sensing technology, granting it a range of motion and the ability to react to the player's actions. In the Pro Edition cabinets, Rath's head features only vertical movement.

The cabinets also feature a wide cast of voice actors, with over 11,000 recorded lines of speech.

The most distinct of the three versions is the Limited Edition cabinet, of which only 740 will be produced. In addition to its distinct cabinet decal, the LE cabinet features a plaque denoting its numerical designation among the 740 produced, the Speaker Expression Lighting System, an upgraded sound system, a full-colored mirrored backglass that was inspired by dragons, a red finish to the body of the cabinet,Described in promotional material as a "'Dragon Red' powder-coated pinball armor". an autograph on the bottom arch by the cabinet's designer, an anti-reflective quality to the playfield glass, and a shaker motor. Beyond the cabinet itself, purchasers were given a signed Certificate of Authenticity.The number of Limited Edition cabinets produced was limited to 740 for the purpose of referencing the last two digits of the year in which Dungeons & Dragons debuted—1974.

Summary
An adventuring company known as the Dragonshield Guild fights to thwart the goddess Tiamat's latest scheme for arising on the Prime Material, which has triggered "the War of Dragons", all the while trying to interfere in the machinations of notorious threats Balinor, Sammaster, and Xanathar.

Gameplay

Going beyond the typical pinball experience, The Tyrant's Eye features three campaigns, playable by up to four players. At the onset of the game, players can select from four distinct race/class combinations with predetermined character statistics. These statblocks include the attributes from Dungeons & Dragons, hit points, armor class, a luck percentile, and four item slots.

 Anya the halfling rogue; receives extra luck & treasure
 Ardor the dragonborn paladin; does extra damage to dragons
 Helenir Weavhart the high elf wizard; shield stays active for longer
 Frey Curlyfoot the dwarf cleric; does extra damage to undead

Three additional characters can be unlocked by beating each of the game's campaigns.
 Goregath the goliath barbarian
 Juniper Amari the wood elf bard
 Rhyme the tiefling ranger

At the onset of each game, pressing the Right flipper selects the character, while pressing the Left flipper selects what location on the map to start at. Plunging the ball will start or resume the campaign with the currently selected character. When resuming a saved campaign, a location can't be selected, instead you automatically start in the last city visited in the previous game session.

Besides the two flippers, there is a central action button that can be pressed to temporarily raise a metal bar between them. Labeled as "shield", this serves to prevent the loss of balls.

Through either adventuring or visiting the game's store, Fizmo's Shoppe, and spending accumulated gold pieces players can obtain access to inventory items.

By hitting Dungeon targets on the playfield or paying 75 gp to Fizmo's Shoppe, players can open up the "Dungeon Crawl". In the Premium & Limited Edition versions, this takes the form of a grated trapdoor or scoop. By shooting a ball over the "Dungeon Crawl" space or into the trapdoor, players access dynamically generated dungeon levels. In the Premium & Limited Edition versions, this trapdoor holds the ball until a dungeon is finished. There are a total of five dungeon levels that change out on a weekly basis. These levels, as well as animated combat encounters and character stats, all play out on the cabinet's LCD screen. The difficulty of encounters scales with the player as they progress and level up.

Fighting enemies during an encounter involves shooting at certain red lights on the playfield. Whether or not an attack connects with an enemy is randomly determined by a dice roll. Initiating combat with Rath requires hitting the three bank targets under the red dragon that represent his treasure horde. This gives players the opportunity to amass gold pieces. In the Premium and Limited Edition models, Rath's head moves from left to right during combat and unleashes multiple balls from its mouth. All versions refer to this as the "Dragon Multiball".

Quests and game modes at each location are denoted by inserts, numbered 1 through 3, with a border colored either blue, orange, purple, or white. By traveling between cities and completing the color matched modes numbered 1 and 2, players unlock the level 3 mode of the same color in either Dragonspear Castle or Gauntlgrym.

Tiny's Dice Game is a timed, points-based mode in the game where the LCD screen displays the members of the Dragonshield Coster gathered in a tavern to play a game where three dice are shook around in a mug and covered before being revealed. To play one must shoot a ball through the spinner directly above the "Dungeon Crawl", situated under a sign labeled "Tiny's Tavern". Doing so will add a number of additional points to the player's score based on the value of their dice in the game mode. The value of a player's dice can be raised by shooting a ball over the "Dungeon Crawl" (or into the scoop) and the time of the mode can be extended by shooting balls over one of three adjacent arrow paths that are lit up purple.

Using integration with Stern's Insider Connected app platform–a system originally created for their Venom cabinet (2023)–copies of the The Tyrant's Eye are able to save and load a player's accumulated experience points, inventory items, and campaign progress across play sessions. For players wanting a more traditional pinball experience, each game can be started without saved progress.

History
The Tyrant's Eye was developed under the codename "ROPE". It was first teased by Stern on December 31, 2024 and received a full detailed announcement the following year on January 3. The Limited Edition soon sold out.

## Dumathoin's Jest

Dumathoin's Jest was a shop in the Upper Trade Lanes of Skullport in the late 14 century DR.

Structure
Dumathoin's Jest was a one-story building in good condition.

Services
Dumathoin's Jest was a well-regarded gem-cutting and appraisal establishment. The shop was known for meticulous craftsmanship and discretion, qualities that attracted both legitimate collectors and those who preferred their transactions remained unrecorded. The shop's services do not come cheaply: a flat fee of 20% of a gem’s assessed value was the charge for services.

Inhabitants
Despite its name, Dumathoin's Jest had little direct connection to the dwarven deity Dumathoin. When asked about the shop's enigmatic name, owner Nora Ketterling offered no explanation to inquirers, responding only with a knowing smile.

## Dungeons & Dragons Online/Toil and Trouble

Toil and Trouble is a paid quest for Dungeons & Dragons Online where the player characters investigate the King's Forest and make a deal with a hag. The adventure was added to the game on April 16, 2025 as part of Update 73's Tavern Tales quest bundle.

Plot
In the aftermath of the Sschindylryn invasion of Eveningstar and Lolth's schemes, the people of Eveningstar are troubled by yet another menace. Mielikki's Grove in the King's Forest is overrun by blights, and Jarra Morand suspects a hag is to blame. Finding Granny Gertrude nearby, the hag actually proclaims her innocence rather than reveling in the disaster, and blames a nasty Red Wizard of Thay who has sacrificed a blessed unicorn and despoiled the shrine. Gertrude in fact is a deal-maker and has a working relationship with Mielikki, and would be happy to deal with Dhovras if not for her agreement preventing her from harming the inhabitants of the grove—which includes the accursed undead unicorn now defending Dhovras.

In order to resolve the situation, Granny Gertrude hires the players to cleanse Mielikki's shrine and save the day, all with the approval of the goddess herself. Once the unfortunate unicorn is put to rest Dhovras is left vulnerable to the peeved Gertrude, who captures the Red Wizard and drags him off for some hag-style justice.

## Dumpy Dumpling

Dumpy Dumpling was once the headliner performing for the Circus of the Last Days sometime in or before the late 15 century DR.

History
Sometime before their visit to Baldur's Gate in the, the Circus of the Last Days had Dumpy Dumpling as the headliner. After performing for a decade with shows filled with firework farts, chicken-snapping, and pie-in-the-ear tricks, the passion for the art left the man. One day, during a show, Dumpy called on a volunteer. A sly man with reddish-hair stepped onto the stage. The volunteer, Norris Greenie of Baldur's Gate, seemed to have charisma enough to inspire pure joy with his command of the stage. He caused riotous laughter with his one liners and more with his witty comebacks. Not just the crowd was mesmerized by the redheaded volunteer, so was the author of Officially Unofficial History of the Circus of the Last Days!. When the show was over, the writer forgot that he was watching Dumpy Dumpling's show and not the red-headed man's. Lucretious, the proprietress of the Circus of the Last Days, quickly realized the volunteer's star quality and recruited him into her troupe. She offered him a job right there on stage to the crowd's thunderous applause. This was how Dribbles the Clown replaced Dumpy Dumpling as the star of the plane-hopping circus from the Feywild.

## Dunsteen

Lord Mage Dunsteen was a wizard of Mlembryn in the early-to-mid 3 century DR.

Description
He was curl-bearded, and dressed splendidly.

Personality
The man was irritable, had a dangerous temper, and was an obscurantist who believed that word of magic was not to be spread.

Abilities
He could cast fireball.

Possessions
He had rings, and splendid robes.

History
During the early-to-mid 3 century DR,Elmara is later seen with the Brave Blades in Marpenoth of the Year of Much Ale (233) and she joined them before the last cold season, which would've meant she joined them in late 232 at the most. Prior to this, Elmara has spent two years ("Seasons") starting at some point after early summer of 229, which starts mid-year. Thus, their encounter most likely occurs either in the second half of 231 or the first half of 232. Dunsteen heard a woman tell stories of magic at an inn in Mlembryn. That woman was a priestess of Mystra, whom he attempted to browbeat into silence; she claimed instead that she was on a holy mission from Mystra, and he attacked her afterwards, as she left the inn, hurling fireballs at her. She responded in kind, and after her victory, she cast a spell that compelled him to speak only the truth. Asking him why he attacked her, he confessed to wanting to keep magic as obscure as possible, so less people would work magic and render his skills more valuable.

Before leaving, she asked him who she should ask to learn more about magic. Smirking, he told her that Ilhundyl of Calimshan would give her the best answer of all.

## Durnibas Uxyndar

Durnibas Uxyndar was a skilled drow arcanist during the 1480s DR.

Description
Durnibas had his white hair worn long and loose.

Abilities
Durnibas was a powerful spellcaster and knew ball lightning, chain lightning, delayed blast fireball, force missiles, Otiluke's freezing sphere, and polar ray.

History
During the Sschindylryn invasion of Eveningstar, Durnibas lurked in the ruins of Riz Malag, not too far from Sschindylryn itself. He studied a pool of water in the ruins, from which he could summon two loyal water elementals.

## Dungeons & Dragons Online/Heart of Netheril

Heart of Netheril is the title of a cancelled adventure pack for Dungeons & Dragons Online initially planned to conclude the Netheril storyline in the Forgotten Realms, introduced in Shadowfell Conspiracy. It would have seen players exploring the desert of Anauroch and the ancient Netherese city of Thultanthar, facing off with Netheril's strongest wizards, the alien phaerimm, and possibly even the dread Tarrasque itself.

## Durilghar Szind

Durilghar Szind was a drow necromancer of House Szind from Sschindylryn in the late 15 century DR.

Description
Durilghar had tied-back white hair and wore an elaborate black robe and skull mask.

Abilities
Durilghar was a powerful necromancer and, in addition to animating and controlling the undead, could cast chain lightning, enervation, finger of death, horrid wilting, necrotic ray, Niac's biting cold, and symbol of weakness.

History
During the Sschindylryn invasion of Eveningstar during the mid-1480s DR, Durilghar raised the dead in the King's Forest so that they could be used as fighters against the defenders of Cormyr. He performed much work at the razed Deep Glen village, where many fresh human corpses could be found.

## Duncan (Ravens Bluff)

Duncan was one of Field General Marcus Rubicon's staff in the Vast in the mid-to-late 14 century DR.

Description
He was dressed in green livery.

History
Alongside his fellow staffer Malcom, Duncan was part of Field General Marcus Rubicon's staff. He was present during the meeting in which the Field General sent adventurers to the nearby protectorate of Stag Falls, as the village's taxes had not been paid, and the patrol he sent to investigate had failed to return. Duncan, personally, briefed the adventurers on the background of Stag Falls itself.

## Dungeons & Dragons Online/Eveningstar Challenge Pack

The Eveningstar Challenge Pack is an adventure pack for Dungeons & Dragons Online where the players can face small combat challenges around Eveningstar in order to gain access to fantastical treasures. The adventure pack was added to the game on June 25, 2012 as part of Update 14, and supplements the concurrent Menace of the Underdark expansion.

Plot
Dryad Grove: The Great Tree involves defending the dryadic Grove of the Great Tree in the King's Forest at the behest of Ravanys Woodsoul, which is under heavy attack by malevolent drow forces. The grove must be protected while it builds up enough energy to blast away the drow and reinvigorate the land.

Dryad Grove: Defenseless has the player repeating a similar feat, though without any supporting defensive trees as the drow forces converge on the Great Tree.

Ruined Keep: The Sunset Ritual sends the player via Yenward Chorster to the ruined keep in the King's Forest, where the War Wizard Arganon Pine is performing a ritual to turn away threats. Before he can complete it, he must be protected from a small army of bandits and werewolves that seek to stop him.

Ruined Keep: The Daybreak Ritual has the player aiding the wizard Zenyff Bravehand as he performs a second ritual at the ruined keep, only to be attacked by waves of furious natural and druidic forces.

Underdark Arena: Fight to the Finish is a gladiatorial fighting pit represented by the duergar agent Djir Garpeth, run by Azena'ar the drider for House Cay'Vorym.

Underdark Arena: Ring of Fire follows from the previous, and has the red dragon Aptoryx get involved. Having taken an interest in the sport of death matches, Aptoryx got his own arena and filled it up with lava.
